

The Division raised concerns with the proposed COA measure, the MSSS, and recommended
the Applicant consider other COA measures to assess trial endpoints.

On March 12, 2019, the Division issued an advice/information request letter reiterating the
recommendations provided in the advice/information request letter dated December 19, 2018.
On December 16, 2019, the Applicant submitted the phase 3 protocol for VP-VLY-686-3401
(Trial 3401, “Motion Syros: A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study to investigate
the efficacy of tradipitant in subjects affected by motion sickness during travel.”
An end-of-phase 2 meeting was held on May 13, 2020 (meeting minutes dated May 21, 2020).
During the meeting, the following comments and recommendations were provided:

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
On February 28, 2023, the Division issued an advice/information request regarding the design of
Trial 3403.


The Division expressed significant concerns with the proposed trial design, and its ability to
generate interpretable data to support a future marketing application. The Division’s concerns
included the open-label, uncontrolled design that lacked a control arm, and the potential for
significant variability in travel events, timing of drug administration, and tradipitant dosing.
Due to these concerns, the Division did not agree that Trial 3403 could serve as an adequate
and well-controlled investigation.



The Division noted that Trial 3403 was the only trial that proposed to evaluate repeated
administration of tradipitant for the proposed indication; however, this trial may not result in
interpretable data due to the trial design.



The Division informed the Applicant that to support an eventual marketing application for
tradipitant as a pharmaceutical therapy for the prevention of vomiting induced by motion, in
addition to the studies conducted thus far, a 6-month toxicity study in a nonrodent species
should be conducted to adequately assess the risk of cumulative toxicity following repeated
short-term exposures to tradipitant.

On November 8, 2023, the Division held a pre-NDA meeting with the Applicant (meeting
minutes finalized on December 8, 2023). The discussion during the pre-NDA meeting, the
Division made the following comments:


The Division raised concerns about the Applicant’s plan to submit results from Trial 2401
and Trial 3401 to support a marketing application. Specifically, for Trial 2401, the Division
noted a lack of control for overall type I error and previously communicated concerns with
the MSSS instrument that was used to measure the endpoints. The Division also noted that
for Trial 3401, the 85-mg tradipitant arm was not statistically significant due to the
prespecified multiple-testing procedure. According to the multiple testing procedure, the
worst nausea severity score for the 170-mg tradipitant arm was tested and failed to show
statistical significance, thus, the percentage of subjects with vomiting in the 85-mg tradipitant
arm was only nominally significant. The Division also expressed concerns that only Trial
3401 evaluated the 85-mg tradipitant arm, which was not assessed in Trial 2401.



The Division recommended that the Applicant finish Trial 3404, a second phase 3 trial
similar in design to Trial 3401, prior to a future NDA submission.



The Division noted that it was premature to agree to the adequacy of the safety database to
support a future marketing application. The Division stated that the assessment of safety
would focus on safety data from the intended use population that received the to-be-marketed
dosage.



The Division stated that the proposed nonclinical safety database may not be adequate to
inform the repeated use of tradipitant for the prevention of vomiting induced by motion. The
Division noted that although vomiting induced by motion may occur intermittently, it is
likely that a patient will require multiple courses of treatment. Thus, it would be important to
adequately characterize the risks of cumulative toxicity following repeated short-term
(b) (4)
exposures to tradipitant. The Division

On October 11, 2024, the Applicant submitted a protocol amendment for Trial 3403, seeking to
modify the duration of treatment with tradipitant from a maximum of 90 dosing days to a
maximum of 180 dosing days and to extend the treatment period from 12 months to 24 months.
A deficiency - potential hold issues letter was issued on November 15, 2024, that stated the
following:
The nonclinical information submitted to the IND supports a maximum treatment
duration of 3 months (i.e., 90 dosing days). Therefore, we are concerned that the IND
does not contain sufficient information to assess the risks to subjects for the proposed
duration of treatment. You have the option to withdraw the protocol amendment or
provide a response by November 21, 2024.

The Applicant replied on November 20, 2024, with the following comments:




The Applicant noted that most subjects (“approximately 80%”) took less than half of the
maximum allowed doses (i.e., 45 doses). The Applicant expected “the majority of
participants in the proposed amendment would be expected to take fewer than 90 doses over
24 months.”
The Applicant stated that their proposed indication is for the “acute prevention of motion
induced vomiting” and referred the Division to other drugs that were approved for an “acute,
infrequent condition” as rationale to support their position that additional nonclinical studies
were not necessary to support the proposed protocol amendment.

On November 26, 2024, the Division provided the following response to the Applicant:
As we had noted in our previous correspondence, we are concerned that the IND does not
contain sufficient information to assess the risks to clinical trial subjects beyond the
maximum of 90 dosing days, regardless of the frequency and/or duration of use.
You stated in your response that “many participants took only a few doses in the 12month period, and the vast majority (approximately 80%) took fewer than half (45) of the
maximum allowed doses.” Therefore, you expect that the majority of subjects will take
no more than 90 dosing days over 24 months, the total treatment duration proposed in the
protocol amendment. Based on the information you provided, if you amend the protocol
to specify that dosing for each clinical trial subject would be limited to a maximum of 90
dosing days over the intended 24-month total treatment duration, that would satisfactorily
resolve the potential hold concern. You also have the option to withdraw the protocol
amendment.

The Division also sent an advice/information request regarding the proposed protocol
amendment for Trial 3403 on November 26, 2024. The following comments were conveyed to
the Applicant:


The Division reiterated concerns about the interpretability of Trial 3403 due to the trial
design (as previously noted) and the concern that Trial 3403 was the only source of data for
repeated administration of tradipitant in the intended use population. The Division noted that
characterizing the safety and efficacy of repeated administration would be important, as most
patients will likely require multiple courses of treatment over time, since vomiting induced
by motion may recur over that patient’s lifetime.



The Division stated their concern that increasing the sample size of Trial 3403 (from 300 to
500) was a missed opportunity to gather interpretable data that could be obtained from a
different trial design.

On December 4, 2024, the Division replied and reiterated the comments from the November 26,
2024, response. The Division also noted that the November 26, 2024, response was intended to
satisfactorily resolve the matter by providing a path forward for the proposed protocol
amendment, based on the information provided by the Applicant. The Division also stated they
were open to a teleconference to discuss the proposed path forward; however, the Division
requested the Applicant clarify whether there are any potential challenges with amending the
protocol to specify a maximum of 90 dosing days.
On December 9, 2024, the Applicant replied with the following communication, and did not seek
a teleconference to discuss the matter.
Vanda believes the body of safety data for tradipitant is sufficient to support the protocol
amendment referenced above. We see no valid scientific basis to conduct additional
lethal studies in dogs as a prerequisite to execute this study. As such, we do not intend to
withdraw or modify the amended protocol.

On December 23, 2024, Trial 3403 was placed on partial clinical hold based on insufficient
information to assess risks to human subjects.40 Trial 3403 could not be continued except as
specified below:
This partial clinical hold applies to the portion of the amendment to Study 3403 which, as
proposed, would have modified treatment with tradipitant from a maximum of 90 dosing
days to a maximum of 180 dosing days. Accordingly, you may enroll new subjects in
Study 3403, and current subjects may continue in Study 3403, for a maximum of 90
dosing days over the course of the proposed 24-month duration.

The available nonclinical data support dosing with tradipitant for up to 90 dosing days. The
nonclinical information submitted to the IND includes a 6-month study in a rodent species and a
3-month study in a nonrodent species, which support the maximum allowable 90 dosing days
regardless of the frequency of use and/or duration of the study. Therefore, the current
amendment (180 dosing days) exceeds the maximum 90 dosing days that is supported by the
information submitted to the IND.

13. Pharmacology Toxicology
Where appropriate, effect margins are reported relative to the clinical Cmax under three different
conditions: following a single dose of 85 mg, following a single dose of 170 mg, or following
repeated dosing (i.e., at steady state) at a dose of 170 mg/day.41 As tradipitant is intended for the
prevention of vomiting induced by motion, an individual may take a single dose or otherwise use
the drug so infrequently that PK parameters from a single dose are most relevant. Alternatively,
an individual may use tradipitant frequently, or many days in a row (e.g., daily on a week-long
cruise, daily during commute to and from work), such that steady-state PK parameters are most
relevant. Thus, multiple safety/effect margins are reported to best represent the wide range of
potential scenarios with tradipitant under the proposed conditions of use.
While safety margins are reported relative to AUC, when possible, other effect or safety margins
in this section are reported relative to HED (assuming a 60 kg adult) at the maximum clinical
dose of 170 mg/day, due to the limitations of the nonclinical and clinical pharmacokinetic data
with tradipitant and its major human metabolites. The review team notes that tradipitant margins
based on HED are likely overestimating safety, based on the known pharmacokinetic properties
(i.e., nonlinear exposure such that large increases in dose level result in marginal changes in
AUC). Therefore, all HED-based margins should be interpreted with caution.

13.1.1. Primary Pharmacology
Tradipitant is a selective, competitive inhibitor of the human cell membrane neurokinin (NK)-1
receptor. Tradipitant binds to the human NK-1 receptor with sub-nanomolar affinity in
cells/tissues from a variety of species, including human. See Section 13.2.1 for additional
pharmacology studies with tradipitant and its metabolites.
In a study in male Beagle dogs, a single oral dose of tradipitant at ≥0.3 mg/kg administered 2
hours prior to challenge with the pro-emetogenic drug, apomorphine, resulted in significantly
decreased incidence of emesis compared to no treatment (less than the maximum clinical dose of
tradipitant of 170 mg [2.8 mg/kg], based on HED).

13.1.2. Secondary Pharmacology
No significant activity (≥50% binding inhibition) of tradipitant was observed against a panel of
74 other receptors, enzymes, and ion channels, including the NK-2 and NK-3 receptors. See
Section 13.2.2 for an additional secondary pharmacology panel.

13.1.3. Safety Pharmacology
See Section 13.2.3 for additional safety pharmacology studies with tradipitant and its
metabolites.
Central Nervous System
In a central nervous system safety pharmacology study in male CD-1 mice, a single oral dose of
tradipitant at 1500 mg/kg resulted in significantly increased spontaneous ambulatory and
nonambulatory activity, mild hyperactivity, and mild hyperthermia, as well as altered sensitivity
to auditory stimuli relative to controls (effect margin of 43-fold relative to the maximum clinical
dose of 170 mg, based on HED). Neurological examinations were also conducted in the 1- and 3month repeat-dose oral toxicity studies in dogs, and no adverse neurological effects were
reported up to the highest tested dose of 1500 mg/kg/day (294-fold the clinical dose).
Respiratory System
In a respiratory safety pharmacology study in male Sprague Dawley rats, single oral doses of
tradipitant up to 1500 mg/kg (85-fold the clinical dose, based on HED) had no significant effects
on respiratory parameters.
Cardiovascular System
Tradipitant significantly inhibited the hERG channel current in vitro, with an IC50 value of
3.91μM (2,299 ng/mL; approximately 27-, 21-, and 7.0-fold the clinical Cmax in healthy

13.1.4. Pharmacokinetics
See Section 13.2.4 for additional pharmacokinetic studies with tradipitant and its metabolites.
Absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies with tradipitant have been conducted
in vitro and in vivo in mice, rats, rabbits, and dogs. Systemic exposure to tradipitant generally
increased less than proportionally with increasing dose and was generally higher in females
compared to males in all species.
Tradipitant was highly bound to plasma proteins in all species.
The major metabolic pathways of tradipitant were different in nonclinical species compared to
humans. All metabolites detected in the human liver microsomes and slices were detected in
preparations from one or more nonclinical species in vitro; however, metabolites M4 and M8
were not detected in liver microsomes or slices from rats and dogs, the species used for toxicity
testing.45
In humans, tradipitant is extensively metabolized, primarily through non-CYP-mediated
processes and secondarily through CYP3A4, CYP2C19, and CYP2C8. The major circulating
metabolites in humans have been identified as M2, M3, M4, and M8, which are produced by
ketone reduction (M2, M4), N-glucuronidation (M8), and/or pyridine N-oxidation (M3, M4).
Tradipitant significantly inhibited transporters BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, and
MATE2-K in vitro, with IC50 values in the micromolar range. Metabolite M2 also significantly
inhibited OATP1B1 and MATE1, while M3 significantly inhibited OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and
MATE1. No significant inhibition of transporters was observed with metabolite M4. Tradipitant
was a weak substrate of P-glycoprotein (P-gp). M2 was not a substrate of P-gp or BCRP, while
M3 and M4 were strong substrates of P-gp and weak substrates of BCRP.
Fecal excretion was the major excretion route for tradipitant-related material following oral
administration of tradipitant.

13.1.5. Repeat-Dose Toxicity
Repeat-dose oral toxicity studies with tradipitant have been conducted in mice (13 weeks), rats
(up to 6 months), and dogs (up to 3 months). Target organs were identified as liver and thyroid.
Again, it should be noted that the HED-derived safety margins stated here are likely

45

Module 2.6.4 Pharmacokinetics Written Summary, Table 26.

97
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
overestimating safety, based on the known pharmacokinetic properties of tradipitant, and these
margins should be interpreted with caution.
In a 13-week toxicity study in albino mice, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 15, 150, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No significant toxicity was reported in this
study. Increased liver weights were observed at ≥150 mg/kg/day compared to controls with no
histopathological correlates. These findings were considered secondary to the CYP450 enzyme
induction also observed in these groups. Exposure to tradipitant increased less than
proportionally with increasing dose in this study. Decreased exposure was observed between
Days 1 and 14 and was attributed to enzyme induction that accelerated clearance of tradipitant.
Based on the lack of adverse effects, the no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) was
considered the highest tested dose of 1500 mg/kg/day (43-fold the maximum clinical dose of 170
mg, based on HED). Notably, only marginal differences in AUC values were observed among all
dosing groups, such that the differences in AUC between 15 and 1500 mg/kg/day (i.e., a 100fold increase in dose) were only 3.4- to 4.1-fold.
In a 3-month toxicity study in Fischer 344 rats, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 15, 150, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No drug-related mortality, clinical signs of
toxicity, body weight or food consumption changes, or ophthalmology findings were reported in
this study. Hemolysis of blood samples from all dose groups significantly compromised
detection and/or assessment of several clinical pathology parameters. Increased gamma-glutamyl
transferase (GGT) activity (up to 2.1-fold control level) was observed in all treated groups. Dosedependent increases in liver weight were reported compared to controls. Slight increases in
thyroid, kidney, and adrenal gland weights were also reported. Per the Applicant, the organ
weight increases were considered secondary to enzyme induction. Histopathological findings of
minimal hepatocellular hypertrophy and minimal multifocal hepatocellular single cell necrosis
were noted at 1500 mg/kg/day. Minimal to mild thyroid hyperplasia was observed in all treated
groups. Histopathological findings in liver and thyroid were also considered secondary to
enzyme induction, based on available information. Exposure to tradipitant did not increase
significantly with increasing dose in this study. Decreased exposure after repeated dosing was
observed in all treatment groups, consistent with typical CYP450 enzyme induction effects.
Based on the lack of adverse findings, the NOAEL was considered the highest tested dose of
1500 mg/kg/day (85-fold the maximum clinical dose, based on HED); however, confidence in
this finding is limited by the inadequate clinical pathology data, data suggesting loss of exposure
to tradipitant after repeated administration, and marginal differences in AUC among all dosing
groups (i.e., similar to 1.9-fold increase in AUC for a 100-fold increase in dose).
In a 6-month toxicity study in Fischer 344 rats, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 15, 150, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No drug-related mortality, clinical signs of
toxicity, body weight changes, ophthalmology findings, urinalysis abnormalities, or gross
pathology findings were reported. A reversible increase in activated partial thromboplastin time
was observed in males at ≥150 mg/kg/day and in females at all doses compared to controls.
Reversible, dose-dependent increases in GGT activity were observed in males at
≥150 mg/kg/day and in females at all doses compared to controls. No other clinical chemistry
findings indicative of liver injury were reported. Significant histopathological findings were
observed in liver, thyroid, and kidney, including minimal to mild hepatocellular hypertrophy at
all doses, minimal thyroid follicular cell hypertrophy in males at 1500 mg/kg/day and in females
at all doses, and an increased incidence of minimal to mild chronic nephropathy at all doses
98
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
compared to controls. Findings in liver and thyroid were considered secondary to metabolic
enzyme induction by the Applicant. Chronic nephropathy appeared to be an age-related finding,
as it was also observed in the control group; however, the increase in incidence/severity noted in
males at 1500 mg/kg/day was considered treatment-related. Considering that all findings were
resolved following the recovery period, no other histopathological findings indicative of hepatic
or renal injury were observed, and there were no findings indicative of renal function
impairment, the toxicological significance of the liver, thyroid, and kidney findings was
considered limited. Systemic exposure to tradipitant decreased markedly with time, especially
over the first 3 months of dosing. The Applicant attributed this to induction of metabolizing
enzymes accelerating the clearance of tradipitant; the review team agrees. Based on the lack of
adverse findings, the NOAEL was considered to be the highest tested dose of 1500 mg/kg/day
(85-fold the maximum clinical dose, based on HED); however, confidence in this finding is
limited by data suggesting loss of exposure to tradipitant after repeated administration and
marginal differences in AUC among all dosing groups (i.e., similar to 1.8-fold increase in AUC
for a 100-fold increase in dose).
In a 3-month toxicity study in Han Wistar rats, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 15, 150, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No drug-related mortality, clinical signs of
toxicity, body weight changes, ophthalmology findings, urinalysis abnormalities, or gross
pathology findings were reported. Dose-dependent increases in GGT activity were observed at
≥150 mg/kg/day and correlated with increased liver and thyroid weights as well as
histopathological findings of hepatocyte hypertrophy, foci of inflammatory cells with hepatocyte
necrosis (with dose-dependent incidence and severity), and hypertrophy of thyroid follicular
cells. These findings were generally similar to the findings in the 3-month study in Fischer rats
and were generally consistent with induction of metabolic enzymes. However, liver findings
were considered to be more severe in this study compared to the 3-month and 6-month studies in
Fischer rats, as foci of inflammatory cells with hepatocyte necrosis were not reported in Fischer
rats at doses up to 1500 mg/kg/day for up to 6 months but were noted in 6/10 males at
150 mg/kg/day, 9/10 males at 1500 mg/kg/day, and 3/10 females at 1500 mg/kg/day in this
study. The reason for the apparent strain-related differences in liver toxicity is unknown.46
Systemic exposure to tradipitant decreased markedly with time. The Applicant attributed this to
induction of metabolizing enzymes accelerating the clearance of tradipitant; the review team
agrees. Based on the adverse findings of necrosis in the liver of males at ≥150 mg/kg/day and
females at 1500 mg/kg/day, the NOAEL was considered the lowest dose of 15 mg/kg/day in
males (less than the maximum clinical dose, based on HED) and the mid dose of 150 mg/kg/day
in females (8.5-fold the maximum clinical dose); however, confidence in this finding is limited
by data suggesting loss of exposure to tradipitant after repeated administration and marginal
differences in AUC among all dosing groups (i.e., 2.2- to 3.1-fold increase in AUC for a 100fold increase in dose).
46

Exposures to tradipitant and M3 were slightly lower in Han Wistar rats compared to Fischer rats following 13
weeks/3 months of dosing at 150 mg/kg/day (the dose at which both strains have data including metabolites M2,
M3, and M4). Cmax values for tradipitant and M3 in Han Wistar rats were 0.6- to 0.7-fold and 0.4- to 0.6-fold those
observed in Fischer rats, respectively. AUC values for tradipitant and M3 in Han Wistar rats were 0.9-fold and 0.4to 0.6-fold those observed in Fischer rats, respectively. Metabolites M2 and M4 were below the limit of
quantification in Han Wistar rats but observed at low levels in Fischer rats. C max and AUC values for tradipitant at
1500 mg/kg/day were similar between the two strains (0.8- to 1.1-fold; metabolites not measured). Therefore,
differences in exposure to tradipitant, M2, M3, and M4 do not explain the strain differences in observed toxicity.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
In a 1-month toxicity study in Beagle dogs, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 50, 200, 800, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No drug-related mortality, clinical signs
of toxicity, or changes in feed consumption, body weight, ophthalmology, neurological
parameters, ECG parameters, hematology and urinalysis parameters, or histopathology were
reported. Treatment resulted in increased liver weights, increased hepatic cytochrome P450
content, macroscopic liver enlargement, and increases in ALT (≥800 mg/kg/day) and ALP (all
dose groups). However, there were no microscopic correlates in the liver, and these effects were
considered likely to be secondary to the observed hepatic CYP450 enzyme induction. No loss of
exposure or accumulation was observed with repeated dosing of tradipitant in this study. Based
on the lack of adverse effects, the NOAEL was considered the highest tested dose of
1500 mg/kg/day (294-fold the maximum clinical dose, based on HED). Notably, only marginal
differences in AUC values were observed among all dosing groups, such that the differences in
AUC between 50 and 1500 mg/kg/day (i.e., a 30-fold increase in dose) were only 1.9- to 4.0fold.
In a 3-month toxicity study in Beagle dogs, tradipitant was orally administered once daily at
doses of 0 (vehicle), 50, 275, or 1500 mg/kg/day. No drug-related mortality or changes in feed
consumption, ophthalmology, neurological parameters, ECG parameters, hematology and
urinalysis parameters, or gross pathology were reported. Drug-related clinical observations
included white material in feces in males and females at ≥275 mg/kg/day and emesis in males at
1500 mg/kg/day. Body weight loss was observed in males at 1500 mg/kg/day (considered
adverse), and decreased body weight gain was reported in males and females at all doses
compared to controls. Minimal to mild, dose-dependent increases in hepatic enzymes (ALP,
GGT, and ALT) were observed at 275 and/or 1500 mg/kg/day with no histopathological
correlates; these findings were considered secondary to hepatic microsomal enzyme induction, as
hepatic CYP450 enzyme induction was observed in the 1-month oral toxicity study in dogs.
Minimal to mild diffuse vacuolation of follicular epithelial cells in the thyroid gland was
observed in females at all doses but not in males; these findings were considered not adverse. No
loss of exposure or accumulation was observed with repeated dosing of tradipitant in this study.
Based on the observed weight loss in males at 1500 mg/kg/day, the NOAEL was considered the
mid dose of 275 mg/kg/day (54-fold the maximum clinical dose, based on HED). Notably, only
marginal differences in AUC values were observed among all dosing groups, such that the
differences in AUC between 50 and 1500 mg/kg/day (i.e., a 30-fold increase in dose) were only
2.3- to 2.6-fold.

13.1.6. Genetic Toxicology
Tradipitant was negative in a complete battery of genetic toxicity studies, including the Ames
assay, an in vitro chromosomal aberration assay in human lymphocytes, and an in vivo
micronucleus assay in mice. See Section 13.2.5 for genotoxicity data with the metabolites.

13.1.7. Carcinogenicity
The carcinogenic potential of tradipitant was evaluated in a 26-week study in CB6F1-TgN
(RasH2) transgenic mice and a 104-week study in Han Wistar rats. In transgenic mice, no tumors
were attributed to treatment with tradipitant at doses up to 1500 mg/kg/day. In male and female
100
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
rats, the incidence of thyroid follicular cell adenomas was significantly higher compared to
controls at the mid and high doses (30 and 150 mg/kg/day or 1.7- and 8.5-fold the maximum
clinical dose, respectively, based on HED; less than the exposure to tradipitant in males and 2.8to 3.4-fold the exposure to tradipitant in females at the MRHD, based on AUC). Combined
incidences of thyroid follicular cell adenoma and carcinoma were significantly higher compared
to controls in male rats at the high dose of 150 mg/kg/day only and in female rats at
≥30 mg/kg/day.

13.1.8. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
No effects on fertility, maternal health, or embryo-fetal development were observed in rats
treated with tradipitant during the periods of mating and organogenesis at doses up to
1000 mg/kg/day (57-fold the maximum clinical dose, based on HED; 2.2-fold based on AUC).
No effects on maternal health or embryo-fetal development were observed in rabbits treated with
tradipitant during the period of organogenesis at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day (114-fold the
maximum clinical dose; 1.4-fold based on AUC). In rats treated with tradipitant during gestation
and lactation, no maternal toxicity or developmental effects on the offspring were observed at
doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day (57-fold the maximum clinical dose; 2.2-fold based on AUC).
In a juvenile animal study in Han Wistar rats, tradipitant was orally administered at doses of 0
(vehicle), 15, 50, or 150 mg/kg/day for 11 weeks, from Postnatal Day 10 to 90. No juvenilespecific toxicities were observed. Dose-dependent increases in liver, thyroid, and parathyroid
weights were observed compared to the controls. Increased liver weights correlated with elevated
liver enzymes and microscopic finding of hepatocellular hypertrophy, while increased thyroid
weights correlated with hypertrophy of the thyroid follicular cells. Consistent with the findings in
adult animals, changes observed in the liver and thyroid were considered secondary to metabolic
enzyme induction.

13.1.9. Special Toxicology
Tradipitant showed little to no light absorption in the 290 nm to 800 nm wavelength range; thus,
the phototoxicity potential of tradipitant was considered low.
See Section 13.2.6 for additional special toxicology studies with tradipitant.

13.2. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With
the New Drug Application
Twenty-three additional primary pharmacology, safety pharmacology, pharmacokinetic, genetic
toxicology, and special toxicology studies with tradipitant and/or its major human metabolites
were submitted to the current NDA without being previously submitted to IND 141315 or crossreferenced from other INDs. Full reviews of these studies were conducted under the current
NDA 220152 (see Nonclinical Supplementary NDA Review dated December 30, 2025).

101
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)

13.2.1. Primary Pharmacology
Tradipitant completely inhibited ligand binding to the human NK-1 receptor in vitro at a
concentration of 10μM. The IC50 values of tradipitant for ligand binding at the NK-1 receptor
were 0.40 and 3.3nM, respectively, in two separate in vitro studies. Tradipitant was also shown
to inhibit substance P-mediated calcium release in HEK293 cells with an IC50 value of 0.63nM.
The four major human metabolites of tradipitant, M2, M3, M4, and M8, have been shown or are
presumed to be pharmacologically active based on available data. All four metabolites inhibited
ligand binding to the NK-1 receptor by ≥99.0% at a concentration of 10µM. M8 was shown to
inhibit ligand binding at the receptor with an IC50 of 0.49nM and to inhibit substance P-mediated
calcium release in vitro with an IC50 of 6.3nM. M2, M3, and M4 were reported by the
Applicant12 to inhibit binding to the NK-1 receptor with IC50 values of 1.55, 0.38, and 2.23nM,
respectively; the functional activity of these metabolites was not tested.

13.2.2. Secondary Pharmacology
Among a panel of 189 receptors, channels, and transporters, tradipitant was shown to
significantly inhibit the rat benzothiazepine and dihydropyridine L-type calcium channels (65%
and 63%, respectively) as well as the rat gamma-aminobutyric acid type A receptor (GABAA) tbutyl bicycloorthobenzoate (TBOB) chloride channel (62%). M2 also significantly inhibited the
rat benzothiazepine L-type calcium channel (52%), human estrogen receptor alpha (ERα; 64%),
and rat GABAA TBOB chloride channel (53%). No significant inhibition of the tested receptors,
channels, and transporters was observed with M3, M4, or M8.

13.2.3. Safety Pharmacology
A potential risk for hepatotoxicity with tradipitant was identified in the completed repeat-dose
toxicity studies (see Section 13.1.5). In the 13-week toxicity study in Han Wistar rats, foci of
inflammatory cells and minimal to slight hepatocyte necrosis were observed in the livers of 6/10
mid dose males, 9/10 high dose males, and 3/10 high dose females (clinical effect margins of 8.5
in males and 85 in females, respectively, relative to the maximum clinical dose of 170 mg, based
on HED). These adverse liver findings were not observed in studies of up to 6 months in Fischer
rats or studies of up to 3 months in dogs; however, nonadverse liver findings attributed to
adaptive effects following induction of metabolic enzymes and/or not correlated with adverse
histopathological findings in the liver were observed in all nonclinical species/strains. It is wellestablished that prolonged exposure to a drug at levels previously shown to be adaptive may
eventually result in liver cell injury due to a failure of adaptive mechanisms. Given the presence
of adaptive changes in all species tested, it is plausible that evidence of hepatotoxicity could be
observed in a chronic toxicity study in Han Wistar rats (i.e., the more susceptible strain) or a
chronic toxicity study in a nonrodent species.
In addition to the in vivo studies, the Applicant later conducted five human cell-based in vitro
safety pharmacology studies47 that further demonstrated the potential for tradipitant and its

previously submitted to and reviewed under NDA 218489. The fifth study, HPTOX-3610, was submitted to NDA
220152 during the review cycle following a nonclinical information request (SDN 9; March 25, 2025).

3D human donor-derived hepatocyte spheroids, and one study was conducted using an in vitro

In the four non-GLP studies using cultured 3D human donor-derived hepatocyte spheroids,

studies, even when the same donors were used. In the initially submitted Studies HPTOX-3550,

be noted that the Applicant did not conduct in vitro to in vivo extrapolation (IVIVE) analyses to
determine how the in vitro concentration relates to that observed in humans, and, thus, the actual
effect margins should be interpreted with caution. Additionally, no potential mechanism for the
observed hepatocyte toxicity was identified in these studies.

Effect margins were calculated by direct comparison of the human Cmax and in vitro concentrations. The Applicant did not conduct

However, comparing in vitro and in vivo concentrations should occur after the establishment of an IVIVE process using in vitro data
to predict in vivo exposure. IVIVE accounts for factors such as drug metabolism, distribution, and integrated physiological processes
within the body to determine an in vitro-in vivo scaling factor. This scaling factor informs how much higher the in vitro concentration
must be compared to the in vivo plasma concentration to cause similar biological effects in target cells. In the absence of such a
threshold to determine whether the assay findings are positive or negative, the effect margins presented here have limited clinical
utility.

Overall, the utility of these in vitro liver safety pharmacology studies is limited due to their short
duration and lack of clear relationship to clinical risk. However, these studies demonstrate that
the potential for liver toxicity is not an animal-specific finding, as toxicity was consistently
observed with human donor-derived hepatocytes. This safety concern was not identified in
clinical trials with tradipitant up to 90 doses (see Section 7.6)

13.2.4. Pharmacokinetics
Tradipitant and its metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were highly bound in vitro using plasma from
rat, mouse, and human. Plasma binding of tradipitant or its metabolites was not affected by
concentration in any species. M8 was not tested for plasma protein binding.
Using recombinant human enzymes, UGT1A4 and UGT2B7 were identified as UGTs capable of
converting tradipitant to the metabolite M8.

Where the definition of minimal toxicity included >95% healthy hepatocyte morphology and <5% dead cells. See

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
In studies using human liver microsomes, tradipitant competitively inhibited CYP3A activity
with an inhibitory constant (Ki) of 0.92μM and noncompetitively inhibited CYP3A activity with
a Ki of 11μM. Tradipitant competitively inhibited both CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 activities with Ki
values of 9.0 and 4.7μM, respectively. Tradipitant also noncompetitively inhibited CYP2C8
activity with a Ki value of 17μM. No significant effects were observed on CYP1A2, CYP2D6,
CYP2E1, or CYP2B6 activity.
In a study using primary human hepatocytes, tradipitant was considered a potential inducer of
CYP3A4 activity in vitro. No biologically significant effects of tradipitant were observed on
CYP1A2 activity.

13.2.5. Genetic Toxicology
Metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were negative for genotoxicity in the Ames assay and in vitro
chromosomal aberration assays using human lymphocytes. M8 was not assessed for
genotoxicity, but the risk is considered low given the structural similarly to tradipitant and the
other major human metabolites, all of which were negative for genotoxicity.

13.2.6. Special Toxicology
The Applicant conducted two special toxicology studies using a “four-organ” MPS comprised of
human hepatocytes, cardiomyocytes, proximal tubular epithelial cells, and cortical neurons, with
the objective of prospectively evaluating the safety of tradipitant in a human-based system.
In the first study, the Applicant stated that no effects of tradipitant were observed on selected
biomarkers of function52 for any of the four cell types following 7 days of treatment when
administered once per day at concentrations of 3 or 100μM. These concentrations were
determined to result in cellular exposure to tradipitant at 500 and 3,500 ng/mL, respectively
(approximately 5.9/41-, 4.5/31-, and 1.5/11-fold the clinical Cmax in healthy volunteers following
administration of a single dose of 85 mg, a single dose of 170 mg,42 and 170 mg/day at steady
state,43 respectively). Importantly, limitations of the assay precluded accurate interpretation of
the study findings (see below).
In the second study, evidence of potential toxicity with tradipitant was observed with select
endpoints under select conditions at select timepoints (up to 14 days) in hepatocytes (substantial
cell detachment), cardiomyocytes (increased troponin-I, decreased cardiac force, increased field
potential duration), renal proximal tubular epithelial cells (increased kidney injury molecule-1
[KIM-1] release), and cortical neurons (increased firing rate). The Applicant concluded that
“chronic” exposure of tradipitant in the four-organ system yielded only “minor adverse effects in
all organ constructs.” When comparing the safety of tradipitant across systems containing either
high, moderate, or low metabolic activity (based only on CYP3A4 activity), significant effects of
metabolic activity could be observed, suggesting that differences in hepatic expression of
metabolizing enzymes (e.g., polymorphisms leading to activity of metabolic enzymes that is
higher or lower than that seen in the general population) may influence physiological outcomes
52

Hepatocytes: CYP3A4 activity, hepatocyte viability, albumin production; cardiomyocytes: cardiac force, beat
frequency, and conduction velocity; renal proximal tubule cells: kidney-injury molecule 1 (KIM-1) release; cortical
neurons: half-peak width activity, spike amplitude, and firing rate.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

NEREUS (tradipitant)
in this test, including outcomes related to safety. As with the first four-organ study, limitations of
the assay precluded accurate interpretation of the study findings (see below).
Based on the available information,53 it appears that the two studies in the four-organ system
were not conducted in compliance with US FDA GLP regulations. The Applicant provided a
(b) (4)
statement from the contract research organization conducting the studies,
indicating that, in their view, US FDA GLP regulations, “did not apply [to] our organization as
we are not doing biomarker or similar analysis but really trying to augment or replace animal
studies.”53 However, based on the stated objectives, the four-organ MPS studies are prospective
in vitro nonclinical laboratory studies intended to support the safety and approval of tradipitant
under this NDA. Therefore, the GLP regulations are applicable.54 The validity and reliability of
the results from nonclinical safety studies depend on careful adherence to certain standards for
study conduct that help to ensure the quality, integrity, and reliability of the data (Carroll 2016),55
establishing confidence in the scientific merit of the study so that accurate interpretation of the
results is possible. Compliance with U.S. FDA GLP regulations or, when appropriate, an
adequate justification of why noncompliance does not affect interpretation or validity of the
study findings, is critical to assure the quality and integrity of the safety data obtained from
nonclinical studies and use these data to assess risk in humans. The lack of U.S. FDA GLP
compliance and absence of adequate justification by the Applicant for noncompliance are
considered limitations of these studies.
In addition to the lack of compliance with U.S. FDA GLP regulations, other limitations in the
design and conduct of these studies preclude their use for regulatory decision-making in this
matter.

NEREUS (tradipitant)


The evaluation of potential toxicity using the four-organ system was limited in scope (i.e.,
not comprehensive) for the reasons including but not limited to:
- The MPS used human-derived cells from the heart, liver, kidney, and brain, a limited
subset of tissues compared to those present in a whole animal or human. Furthermore,
there was no rationale for the specific subset of organs chosen for these studies or how an
investigation using cells from these organs can evaluate toxicity within fully integrated
physiological systems (i.e., including any effects of metabolism, immune responses, and
hormones) to provide a broad, tissue-agnostic assessment of toxicity and to inform safe
use of a drug.
- Within an individual “organ,” only a single cell type was cultured and exposed to
tradipitant. A single cell type only represents a portion of an organ and ignores the unique
and functionally important architecture of the organ; thus, such a construct cannot
adequately or comprehensively assess potential toxicities within that organ, precluding a
recapitulation of full in vivo physiology and pathophysiology.
- These studies evaluated a small number of endpoints (one to three factors per “organ”)
which do not provide enough information to evaluate potential toxicity in each tissue.



The biological relevance of the system or the chosen endpoints to support the safety of
tradipitant was neither justified nor established.
- Some evaluated endpoints (1) are not considered representative of toxicity (i.e., CYP
activity), (2) were used as biomarkers of organ injury despite a lack of validation or
clinical acceptance (i.e., KIM-156), and/or (3) were inconsistent with the results of other
studies, including the in vitro liver assays described above and the completed toxicity
studies in rats and dogs (i.e., CYP activity, hepatocyte viability).
- Positive controls were either not used to demonstrate assay validity and potential toxicity
in this system using the chosen endpoints (Study HSTOX-7619), or the utility of the data
generated with the positive control compounds was limited due to lack of effects or overt
cytotoxicity limiting measurement of the specific endpoint (Study HSTOX-2008-002).
- As acknowledged by the Applicant, the metabolism of tradipitant in the four-organ
system was significantly different than that observed in humans or in nonclinical species.
Although the Applicant attempted to address this issue by using three hepatocyte donors
with differing metabolic activities in Study HSTOX-2008-002, unexplained differences
in metabolism remained compared to data from nonclinical and clinical studies.
- These studies were conducted using a single donor for each primary tissue type (with the
exception of the liver construct in Study HSTOX-2008-002). Any findings in these

56

KIM-1 has not been qualified as a stand-alone biomarker of kidney injury for any context of use. Urinary KIM-1
has been qualified as part of a panel of six urinary biomarkers (clusterin, cystatin-C, KIM-1, N-acetyl-beta-Dglucosaminidase, neutrophil gelatinase-associated lipocalin, and osteopontin) whose composite measure is used in
conjunction with traditional measures to aid in the detection of kidney tubular injury in phase 1 trials in healthy
volunteers when there is an a priori concern that a drug may cause renal tubular injury in humans. See FDA (2020).
A similar panel of urinary biomarkers (KIM-1, albumin, total protein, β2-microglobulin, cystatin C, clusterin, and
trefoil factor-3) has been qualified for the detection of acute drug-induced nephrotoxicity in rats (in conjunction with
traditional clinical chemistry markers and histopathology) in toxicology studies. See FDA (2009). Another
biomarker panel including urinary KIM-1 is in the qualification process.

assays may reflect the response of an individual donor and may not be predictive of the
general population.
- The duration of treatment in these studies was only 7 or 14 days; short-duration
experiments do not provide data on the chronic toxicity of the drug.
Based on these limitations, the four-organ studies as designed, conducted, and reported do not
contribute to the assessment of unrestricted use of tradipitant over a lifetime for the prevention of
vomiting induced by motion.

13.2.7. Other Toxicology
In silico analyses of tradipitant and metabolites M2, M3, M4, and M8 were conducted using
various tools and databases and provided in a 2015 report.57 Although potential toxicities with
tradipitant and/or its major metabolites were identified, this nonclinical test was conducted to
assess “the biochemical and biomedical properties of tradipitant and four metabolites,” not as a
prospective assessment to determine the safety of tradipitant nor as a supportive study to further
understand the safety of tradipitant to support a marketing application. Therefore, the findings in
this report are not considered scientifically relevant for this application.
Updated in silico analyses were conducted by the Applicant to support the safety of metabolites
M2, M3, and M4 for this application.58,59,60,61 These analyses were intended to assess if and how
structural differences in the metabolites might affect their toxicity profile compared to
tradipitant. Briefly, these in silico analyses were conducted using a machine learning-assisted
workflow developed by xAI's Grok system, an advanced large language model trained on
scientific datasets and further validated via the Applicant’s in-house Python scripts. Notably, the
Applicant’s comparative structural prediction data were inferred based on RDKit-computed
descriptors and qualitative thresholds aligned with best practices from consensus classifiers,
rather than direct execution of these models. It is not clear why the Applicant chose to design a
workflow using a large language model and a custom set of molecular descriptors and use
information from existing classifier techniques to ‘infer’ predictions, rather than directly using
existing (Q)SAR modeling software. Additionally, insufficient detail was provided on the
consensus classifiers used in the analyses to fully define the approach. However, enough
information was provided to facilitate review of the findings.
For metabolite M2, no additional reactive groups were identified compared to tradipitant, and no
significant differences between tradipitant and M2 were predicted for PK parameters, acute
toxicity, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, aquatic toxicity, or skin sensitization. The potential for
tradipitant and M2 to cause nephrotoxicity and neurotoxicity was also considered comparable
and low. Compared to tradipitant, M2 was predicted to have slightly increased risk of
hepatotoxicity (increased oxidation potential, potential drug-induced liver injury [DILI] alerts)
and cardiotoxicity (increased hERG affinity, with predicted IC50 >5µM or approximately 29-fold

the clinical Cmax,ss). Despite the slight increase in risk presented by these analyses, the potential
liver toxicity of M2 is considered to be consistent with tradipitant based on the CDER (Q)SAR
analyses and findings in the completed in vitro liver safety pharmacology studies. The potential
for M2-related cardiotoxicity due to hERG channel binding, while not directly assessed in

(b) (4)
clinical TQT study
Metabolite M3 was shown to have structural alerts for oxidative stress, hepatotoxicity, and
genotoxicity related to the N-oxide moiety and increased covalent binding risk (e.g., sensitization
and hepatotoxicity) related to the alpha, beta-unsaturated ketone moiety that are not present in
tradipitant. Compared to tradipitant, M3 had no significant differences predicted for PK
parameters, acute toxicity, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, aquatic toxicity, or skin sensitization.
The potential for tradipitant and M3 to cause nephrotoxicity and neurotoxicity was also
considered comparable and low. M3 was predicted to have slightly increased risk of oxidative
stress, hepatotoxicity, and cardiotoxicity compared to tradipitant.
For metabolite M4 compared to tradipitant, no significant differences were predicted for PK
parameters, acute toxicity, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, aquatic toxicity, or skin sensitization.
The potential for tradipitant and M4 to cause nephrotoxicity and neurotoxicity was also
considered comparable and low. Compared to tradipitant, M4 was predicted to have slightly
increased risk of oxidative stress (structural alert based on the N-oxide), hepatotoxicity (with
potential DILI alerts) and cardiotoxicity (increased hERG affinity, with predicted IC50>5µM or
approximately 29-fold the clinical Cmax,ss). Despite the slight increase in risk presented by these
analyses, the potential liver toxicity of M4 is considered to be consistent with tradipitant based
on the CDER (Q)SAR analysis and findings in the completed in vitro liver safety pharmacology
studies. The potential for M4-related cardiotoxicity due to hERG channel binding, while not
directly assessed in nonclinical studies, has been assessed in completed clinical studies with no
risk identified, although the high-exposure scenario has not been addressed, and will be further
(b) (4)
assessed in a clinical TQT study
Although a structural alert for genotoxicity was identified based on the N-oxide, both
M3 and M4 were demonstrated to be negative for genotoxicity in the Ames assay and an in vitro
chromosomal aberration assay in human lymphocytes.
Overall, the updated in silico analyses predicted that metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were unlikely
to present additional toxicity concerns compared with tradipitant. The review team acknowledges
that this type of computational toxicology analysis is not validated for this intended use.
However, in conjunction with the internally conducted (Q)SAR analysis and following
consideration of all available data, we generally agree with the Applicant’s stated position.

13.2.8. Qualification of Metabolites
The Applicant has identified four major metabolites of tradipitant in human plasma: M2, M3,
M4, and M8. These metabolites have been investigated in a series of nonclinical studies, and
their plasma levels were measured in select toxicity studies conducted in rats and mice. Based on
available data, these metabolites are considered to be pharmacologically active, toxicologically
relevant, and present at significant levels compared to the parent drug, tradipitant. These factors
support that the safety of levels of these metabolites should be qualified based on nonclinical
110
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
data. However, only M3 was present in tested nonclinical species at sufficient levels to be
qualified for safety. M2, M4, and M8 are disproportionate human metabolites.
Using a WoE approach deemed appropriate for this drug and this application, the review team
determined that, based on the in silico structural comparisons in conjunction with the available
pharmacological, PK, and toxicological information about tradipitant and its metabolites, the
metabolites are not expected to result in differing or exaggerated toxicity compared to tradipitant.
Therefore, qualification of M2, M4, and M8 using total drug-related exposure, rather than on an
individual basis, was deemed scientifically appropriate, and the levels of these metabolites were
considered qualified based on this approach.
Therefore, all identified major human metabolites of tradipitant are considered qualified for
safety based on available nonclinical data. However, as the nonclinical data supporting the safety
of unrestricted use of tradipitant over a lifetime are considered inadequate, the safety of the
metabolites is likewise not considered to be adequately supported by nonclinical data for
unrestricted use of tradipitant over a lifetime.

13.2.9. Excipients
There are no novel excipients or excipients of human or animal origin used in the manufacturing
of the tradipitant drug product.
All excipients used in the manufacture of tradipitant capsules are at levels below those used in
previously approved, orally administered products as reported in the FDA Inactive Ingredient
Database (Table 42). The proposed commercial drug product is size 1, white opaque body and
black opaque cap, hard gelatin capsules, printed with 'VANDA 85 mg' in white on the cap,
containing 85 mg of tradipitant per capsule. The gelatin capsule consists of gelatin, titanium
dioxide, FD&C Blue #1, FD&C Red #40, FD&C Yellow #6, and white ink. There are no
nonclinical safety concerns with the composition of the capsule.

13.2.10. Impurities
Overall, there are no safety concerns with the levels of impurities in tradipitant, and all
impurities are adequately controlled. Notable impurities in tradipitant include potentially
genotoxic impurities, related substances, residual solvents, elemental impurities, and potential
nitrosamines.
The Applicant identified six impurities which were considered potentially genotoxic and are
controlled as such at the drug substance level (Table 43). Notably, three of these impurities were
considered International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) M7 Class 5 (i.e., nonmutagenic
impurities) based on the available information (April 2023), including Ames assay, expert rule
and statistical-based bacterial mutagenicity predictions, and/or expert review; however, the
Applicant is controlling these impurities as though they were genotoxic (i.e., Class 2 or 3). We
have no safety concerns with this approach.

Additionally, as there are more than two Class 2 or Class 3 genotoxic impurities in tradipitant,
total genotoxic impurities are controlled in the drug substance at a level of NMT (b) (4) ppm
(equivalent to (b) (4) /day based on the clinical dose of tradipitant of 170 mg/day). Although not
necessary, the Applicant has set this specification to include the total level of all impurities they
have categorized as potentially genotoxic, including the Class 5 impurities listed in Table 43; we
have no safety concerns with this approach. As this level is less than the ICH M7 AI of 5 μg/day
for total genotoxic impurities in drugs with expected lifetime exposure (April 2023), there are no
safety concerns with the levels of total genotoxic impurities in tradipitant.

14. Clinical Pharmacology
14.1. In Vitro Studies
Note that in this review, drug code names LY686017 or VLY-686 are also used to refer to
tradipitant. Thirteen in vitro study reports were submitted characterizing protein binding,
metabolism, and DDI potential. A summary of in vitro studies is provided in Table 44. All in
vitro studies were reviewed during current NDA review as well as during review of
NDA 218489.
A summary of the in vitro DDI assessments for tradipitant is listed in Table 44 and Table 45. For
in vitro DDI assessment, Cmax from Trial 1102 were utilized. In Trial 1102, following 85 mg
single dose for 1 day and 85 mg BID dose for 13 days, the observed mean Cmax was 327 (±42.4)
ng/mL, in healthy subjects.

The plasma protein binding was >99% for M2, 94.6->99% for M3, and 92.8%-98.3% for M4.

Metabolism

There was no evidence for notable concentration-dependent binding in all species with tradipitant, M2,
M3, and M4.
The in vitro metabolism of tradipitant (LY686017) was studied by incubation with human, rat, dog,
primate, and gerbil liver microsomes; and human, rat, dog, and primate liver slice incubations. Overall,
a total of 17 major metabolites and 29 total metabolites were detected in the various matrices.

ADME Report
01

M2 was detected as the major metabolite in human liver microsomes. The N-glucuronide conjugate of
the reduced ketone (M9) was the major metabolite detected in human liver slices. Other significant
metabolites observed in the human liver slices were M2, M4 (N-oxide of the reduced ketone), and an
N-glucuronide metabolite, M8. M8, a glucuronide, was detected only in human material. In the mass
balance study in humans, all major metabolites were <10% of total drug exposure (H8R-MC-HJAK).
In vitro studies indicated that tradipitant (LY686017) is metabolized to M2 (2081070) in incubations with 2004IV-EI002
human liver microsomal samples by non-CYP-mediated processes. Further metabolism of M2 appears
to be CYP-mediated. Tradipitant is metabolized to M3 (21954113) mainly by CYP3A4, although
CYP2C19 and CYP2C8 are also capable of forming M3.
In incubations with recombinant human UGT (rhUGT) enzymes, conversion of tradipitant to M8 was
XT164094
observed with rhUGT1A4 at 5 and 50μM tradipitant, and with rhUGT2B7 at 50μM tradipitant, indicating
UGT1A4 and UGT2B7 are capable of converting tradipitant to M8.

Less than 50% direct inhibition was observed for UGT1A4 activity (maximum of 7.3%) by tradipitant
(0.001 to 1μM) and the IC50 value was reported as >1μM.
The catalytic activities selective for CYP2E1 and CYP2B6 were not substantially inhibited by
LY686017. LY686017 noncompetitively inhibited the biotransformation of paclitaxel to 6ahydroxypaclitaxel, a marker activity for CYP2C8, yielding an apparent Ki value of 17μM.

CD-0822

The biotransformation of bupropion to hydroxybupropion, a marker for CYP2B6 activity, was not
substantially inhibited by LY686017, yielding <45% inhibition over a range of LY686017 concentrations
from 0.009μM to 20μM.
The CYP3A mediated biotransformation of midazolam was competitively inhibited by LY686017,
21-250-TC; 22-250-TC
yielding a Ki value of 0.92μM in vitro. Testosterone metabolism, an additional marker activity for
CYP3A, was noncompetitively inhibited by LY686017, yielding a K i value of 11μM. LY686017 was
found to competitively inhibit both CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 mediated metabolism yielding Ki values of
9μM and 4.7μM, respectively.
Metabolism mediated by CYP1A2 and CYP2D6 was not significantly inhibited by LY686017, exhibiting
≤35% and ≤41% inhibition, respectively, over a LY686017 concentration range of 0.15μM to 200μM.
VLY-686 directly inhibited CYP3A4/5 (testosterone 6β-hydroxylation and midazolam 1′-hydroxylation) XT135114
with IC50 values of 3.3μM and 1.2μM, respectively. VLY-686 may be a metabolism-dependent inhibitor
of CYP3A4/5 and the inhibition is irreversible. There was evidence of weak direct inhibition of CYP2C8
(24.9%) and CYP2C9 (28.6%) by VLY-686 at 5μM concentration. There was little or no evidence of
direct inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C19, or CYP2D6 by VLY- 686. There was little or no
evidence of time-dependent or metabolism dependent inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8,
CYP2C9, CYP2C19, or CYP2D6 by VLY-686.

Study/Report
LY686017 is not likely to be a clinically significant inducer of CYP1A2; however, clinically significant
2003IV-HI02,
induction of CYP3A is possible.
Amend 01
In vitro evaluation of VLY-686 as a potential inducer of cytochrome P450 expression and activity in
XT133124
cultured human hepatocytes indicated that treatment with up to 25μM VLY-686:
 Had little or no effect on CYP1A2 activity and mRNA (≤2-fold change and/or ≤20% compared to the
positive control).
 Caused concentration-dependent increases in CYP2B6 activity and mRNA (up to 15- and 8-fold
change in activity and mRNA, respectively).
 Caused inconsistent responses of CYP2C8 activity and mRNA levels in the three cultures:
concentration-dependent decreases (0.3-fold change or 72% decrease compared to the vehicle
control); little or no change (<2-fold change), or a concentration-dependent increase (up to 3.7-fold
increase and 33% as effective as the positive control) in CYP2C8 activity with concentrationdependent increases in CYP2C8 mRNA levels (up to 3.8-fold change and more effective than the
positive control).
 Had little or no effect on CYP2C9 activity and mRNA levels in two of the three cultures (<2-fold
change) and a concentration-dependent increase in CYP2C9 mRNA levels (up to 4.66-fold and more
effective than the positive control) in one of the three cultures.
 Caused concentration-dependent decreases (down to 0.1-fold change or 88% decrease compared
to the vehicle control) in CYP2C19 activity with little or no change in mRNA levels in two cultures
(<2-fold change), while the third culture had an increase in mRNA levels (2.8-fold change) at 10μM
VLY-686.
 Caused a bell-shaped trend in CYP3A4/5 activity (highest was up to 5.4-fold change and 40% as
effective as the positive control; and lowest was down to 0.4-fold change or a 61% decrease
compared to the vehicle control) and concentration-dependent increases in CYP3A4 mRNA (up to
8.5-fold change and more effective than the positive control).

of 2.11, which was reduced in the presence of the BCRP inhibitor, Ko143. M4 was not a substrate of
ATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, or MATE2-K.

14.2. In Vivo Studies
This NDA submission consists of 27 clinical and clinical pharmacology studies, including 11
phase 1 studies in healthy subjects, 1 phase 2 and 2 completed phase 3 studies and one ongoing
open-label phase 3 study in subjects with history of motion sickness, and 12 studies in subjects
with other conditions. In addition, seven population PK analysis and exposure-response analysis
reports, and one pharmacogenetic report were submitted and reviewed. The proposed to-bemarketed formulation (i.e., 85 mg capsules) was used in seven trials.
Note that in this review, drug code names LY686017 or VLY-686 are also used to refer to
tradipitant. For phase 1 clinical pharmacology studies H8R-EW-HJAE, H8R-FW-HJAN,
VPVLY-686-1101, and H8R-MC-HJAF, considering the nature of study design and objectives,
including the use of preliminary formulations, different dosing regimen, and different population,
these four clinical trials are not considered relevant to the proposed indication and/or proposed
dosage and therefore, are not discussed in this review. The clinical pharmacology studies
reviewed for the current NDA are listed in Table 46. Refer to the OCP review memo in
DARRTS for details.

samples have been measured using adequately validated high performance LC-MS/MS assays in
clinical pharmacology studies. All clinical samples were analyzed within the established stability
periods.

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
14.5.1. Executive Summary
The Applicant’s initial population pharmacokinetic (PPK) analysis (submitted on
December 30, 2024) included no PK data from subjects with motion sickness. An information
request (IR) was sent on April 2, 2025, requesting the Applicant conduct additional PPK
modeling and simulation analysis incorporating the PK data for tradipitant and its metabolites
(M2, M3, M4, and M8) in subjects with motion sickness obtained from Trial 3404 and 3403 (if
124
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
available). In response to the IR, an updated PPK report was submitted on July 22, 2025, based
on pooled tradipitant and metabolite concentration data from 7 trials (1002, 1102, 1301, 2301,
3301, 3403, and 3404). Key highlights of the PPK models are described below:






The Applicant’s NONMEM executions finished with unsuccessful minimization, probably
due to the over-parameterization of the models. However, the PPK analysis generally
captured the concentration data reasonably well and the predicted tradipitant and metabolite
exposures appear generally reasonable.
The observed PK exposures of tradipitant and metabolites of Trial 3403 are higher than other
trials (including Trial 3404, another trial in motion sickness). Per the Applicant, these
differences may reflect the small number of postdose samples obtained in each trial and the
short interval of these samples relative to dosing.
In addition, each dosing amount was derived via averaging the total dosing micrograms over
a period of time for subjects with poor compliance (as low as 46.4%) added uncertainty to
the PPK modeling and simulation analysis. It might be rational to exclude PK data from
subjects with confirmed low compliance in the PPK analysis. The FDA reviewer’s
independent PPK analysis of tradipitant suggested one-compartmental model could also be
used to describe the PK data (with low-compliance subject and Trial 1002 excluded).
However, this model also under-predicted the elevated concentrations observed in Trial
3403.

See the OCP review memo in DARRTS for additional details.

16. Efficacy
16.1. Subgroup Analyses by Demographic
Characteristics
Subgroup analyses were conducted to assess the potential for differences in the treatment effect
on vomiting for various demographic characteristics for Trials 3401 and 3404, as detailed in
Table 48 and Table 49, respectively. Overall, for Trial 3401, the percentage of subjects with
vomiting was numerically lower in each tradipitant arm compared to placebo across almost all
subgroups with the exception of a few subgroups that had very small sample sizes. For Trial
3404, the percentage of subjects with vomiting was generally lower in both tradipitant arms
compared to placebo across most subgroups with a reasonable sample size. In the Black or
African American subgroup, the placebo arm had a lower percentage of vomiting than either
tradipitant arm. However, due to the small sample size in this subgroup, statistical conclusions

NEREUS (tradipitant)
cannot be made. In addition, this trend was not observed in the Black or African American
subgroup in Trial 3401.

17.1. Safety Results, Trial 3401
17.1.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse
Events Summary, Trial 3401
Table 50 below provides an overview of TEAEs that occurred during the evaluation period of
Trial 3401 (i.e., during the vehicle travel assessment). There were no deaths and no SAEs
reported in the tradipitant or placebo arms. There were no TEAEs that led to study
discontinuation. Overall, TEAEs were reported in a greater proportion of subjects in the
tradipitant 85-mg arm (26.8%) and the tradipitant 170-mg arm (28.3%) compared to the placebo
arm (9.0%). The majority of TEAEs were of mild severity.

17.1.2. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Trial
3401
Table 51 below describes TEAEs occurring in ≥2% of subjects and higher in those receiving
tradipitant compared to placebo in Trial 3401. The TEAEs that occurred at a higher rate in the
tradipitant 85-mg arm compared to the placebo arm included somnolence (6.5% versus 0.8%),
headache (5.7% versus 2.5%), fatigue (4.1% versus 0%), dizziness (4.1% versus 1.6%), and
abdominal discomfort (2.4% versus 0%). The TEAEs that occurred at a higher rate in the
tradipitant 170-mg arm compared to the placebo arm included somnolence (8.3% versus 0.8%),
headache (8.3% versus 2.5%), and fatigue (8.3% versus 0%).

TEAEs of somnolence, headache, and fatigue occurred more commonly in both tradipitant arms
compared to the placebo arm. Dizziness occurred at a higher rate than placebo only in the
tradipitant 85-mg arm (4.1% versus 1.6%). Of note, the TEAEs reported by subjects could be
due to motion sickness, as motion sickness may present with a variety of symptoms (e.g., nausea,
vomiting, belching, diaphoresis, dizziness, headache, fatigue, and somnolence). However, the
TEAEs described above were likely related to tradipitant, as they occurred more frequently in the
tradipitant arms than the placebo arm and are consistent with the mechanism of action of
tradipitant (i.e., centrally-acting NK-1 receptor antagonist).

Overall, TEAEs were reported in a greater proportion of subjects in the tradipitant 85-mg arm
(31.7%) and tradipitant 170-mg arm (34.9%) compared to the placebo arm (26.4%). The
majority of TEAEs were of mild severity.

17.3. Safety Results, Trial 2401
Subjects in Trial 2401 were assigned to take a single dose of either tradipitant 170 mg or placebo
during the evaluation phase. All subjects in the safety population were exposed to a single dose
of trial drug.

17.3.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse
Events Summary, Trial 2401
Table 54 below provides a summary of TEAEs reported in Trial 2401. There were no deaths and
no SAEs reported in the tradipitant or placebo arms. No subjects withdrew due to TEAEs.
Overall, TEAEs were reported in a slightly lower proportion of subjects in the tradipitant 170-mg
arm (66.7%) compared to the placebo arm (73.0%). The majority of TEAEs were of mild
severity. The rates of TEAEs in both arms of Trial 2401 are higher than in the phase 3 trials, due
to “motion sickness” being reported as a TEAE.

TEAEs of somnolence, headache, fatigue, and nausea occurred more commonly in the tradipitant
arm compared to the placebo arm. Of note, the TEAEs reported by subjects could be due to
motion sickness, as motion sickness may present with a variety of symptoms (e.g., nausea,
vomiting, belching, diaphoresis, dizziness, headache, fatigue, and somnolence). However, the
TEAEs described above were likely related to tradipitant, as they occurred more frequently in the
tradipitant arms than the placebo arm and are consistent with the mechanism of action of
tradipitant (i.e., centrally-acting NK-1 receptor antagonist).

20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
Not applicable.

21. Other Drug Development Considerations
21.1. Clinical Outcome Assessment
In both phase 3 trials, motion-induced vomiting and nausea symptoms were assessed using the
VA and NA instruments, respectively. The VA is a single-item patient-reported questionnaire
that assesses the incidence of vomiting on a Yes/No response scale. The NA is a single-item
patient-reported questionnaire that assesses nausea severity at its worst on a 5-point Likert scale,
with score ranging from 0 to 4, where 0 = none, 1 = mild, 2 = moderate, 3 = severe, and 4 = very
132
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5720419

NDA 220152
NEREUS (tradipitant)
severe. Subjects completed the VA and NA approximately every 30 minutes throughout the boat
travel.
The VA and NA questionnaires are simplistic measures that appear straightforward for patients
to complete and comprehend. Based on the intrinsic construction of the instruments, the VA and
NA questionnaires are deemed appropriate for assessing motion-induced vomiting
presence/absence and nausea severity, respectively, in the target patient population.
The primary endpoint for both phase 3 trials is constructed as an event-driven measure based on
a “Yes” response on the VA instrument. It indicates the presence or absence of vomiting and
inherently represents a clinically meaningful outcome for patients. In addition, the nausea
component of the secondary endpoint in Trial 3404, defined as worst nausea ≥3 on the NA
instrument, represents severe to very severe symptoms and appears to be clinically meaningful.
Anchor-based analysis is not deemed necessary to interpret the meaningfulness of the primary
endpoint results as this endpoint indicates the presence or absence of vomiting and is inherently
clinically important to patients.

21.2. Abuse Potential
The Applicant provided an Abuse Liability Justification Report and the Controlled Substance
Staff (CSS) reviewed this report to evaluate the abuse potential of tradipitant. Tradipitant was
evaluated for its primary pharmacology in vitro using receptor binding assays and determined to
be a potent, selective, NK-1 receptor inhibitor. Tradipitant was also determined to have no
significant activity (≥50% inhibition) in a broad panel of receptors, transporters, ion channel
receptors, and enzymes associated with abuse. Based on these in vitro findings, additional studies
including nonclinical abuse potential assessments and a human abuse potential study were not
requested. In addition, AEs associated with abuse from the clinical development program were
reviewed. This analysis indicated that the most prevalent AEs observed in clinical trials were
somnolence, headache, and fatigue. CSS notes that these AEs are not indicative of abuse in the
absence of other events such as euphoria, and feeling high, among others. There were no
concerning reports of AEs that suggest that tradipitant has a potential for abuse or physical
dependence. Based on these data, CSS provides the following recommendations:


Tradipitant does not appear to have a potential for abuse and does not require scheduling
under the Controlled Substances Act.



Tradipitant does not require a Section 9 (Drug Abuse and Dependence section) in its label,
should tradipitant be approved.

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for
major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 56). The PI was
reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate)
with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information
needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for
the healthcare practitioner.

BOXED WARNING
Not applicable
The indication was modified from “acute prevention of vomiting
induced by motion” to “prevention of vomiting induced by motion.”
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE
The word “acute” is not necessary as vomiting induced by motion
is a self-limited condition.
 Because the available safety data do not support the
unrestricted use of tradipitant, a statement that the safety of
NEREUS for the prevention of vomiting induced by motion in
adults for more than 90 doses has not been established in
clinical trials was added. Refer to review Section 1.1 and
Section 7.7.
 The recommended dosage was specified to indicate that the
drug should be used “as a single oral dose.” This reflects how
the drug was assessed in the clinical trials that support its
safety and efficacy.
 The recommendation to “[u]se the lowest effective dose” was
added as a result of the dose-dependent increased risk of
adverse events observed in the clinical trials.
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION

modification was to avoid the potential for an individual to
take 85 mg twice in a 24-hour period, which was not
evaluated in the vomiting induced by motion clinical
development program.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
The following postmarketing requirements (PMRs) will be issued:
PMR 4946-1

Conduct a clinical trial to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of an ageappropriate formulation of tradipitant in pediatric patients 2 to <18 years of
age with history of motion sickness.
Final Protocol Submission: June 2026
Study Completion: December 2026
Final Report Submission: March 2027
* Milestone dates were extended from the dates agreed upon in the iPSP to
allow additional time to reach agreement on a final protocol.

PMR 4946-2

Conduct a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled clinical trial to
evaluate the safety and efficacy of tradipitant in pediatric patients 2 to <18
years of age with a history of motion sickness.
Final Protocol Submission: January 2027
Study Completion: January 2028
Final Report Submission: June 2028
* Milestone dates were extended from the dates agreed upon in the iPSP to
allow additional time to reach agreement on a final protocol.

PMR 4946-3

Collect data from a prospective pregnancy exposure registry, preferably a
disease-based multiproduct pregnancy registry, using a cohort analysis that
compares the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to
tradipitant regardless of indication during pregnancy with unexposed
comparator population(s) in a timely manner. Align the study protocol with
protocol(s) outside the U.S. to reach the target sample size. The registry should
identify and record pregnancy complications, major and minor congenital
malformations, spontaneous abortion, stillbirths, elective terminations, preterm
births, small-for-gestational-age births, and any other adverse outcomes,
including postnatal growth and development. Outcomes described in the
protocol will be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including
effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least
the first year of life.
Draft Protocol Submission: June 2026
Final Protocol Submission: December 2026
Study Completion: December 2036
Final Report Submission: June 2037

insufficient nonclinical data were an approvability issue for the originally proposed
unrestricted use. Based on the available safety data, this application is recommended for

of tradipitant for this indication. Refer to Sections 1 and 2.
