Pharmacology/ Toxicology Summary
Only studies reviewed in this document are summarized in the following sections.
Pharmacology (primary & secondary/ MoA)
Key Findings:
The inhibitory potential of tradipitant and metabolites M2, M3, M4, and M8 were
evaluated in a series of in vitro binding assays. Tradipitant, M2, M3, M4, and M8 all
inhibited binding to the human tachykinin NK-1 receptor by ≥ 99.0% at 10 µM.
Tradipitant inhibited binding to the NK-1 receptor with an IC50 of 3.3 nM, while M8
inhibited binding to the receptor with an IC50 of 0.49 nM. The Applicant reported similar
IC50 values for the other metabolites, although these were not included in the provided
study reports.
In functional assays, tradipitant and M8 inhibited substance P-mediated calcium release
with IC50 values of 0.63 and 6.3 nM, respectively. These data confirm that M8 is
pharmacologically active at the NK-1 receptor. Functional assays were not conducted
6
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

with M2, M3, or M4. Based on data from binding assays and their structural similarity to
tradipitant, M2, M3, and M4 are presumed to be active metabolites as well.
Tradipitant significantly inhibited the rat benzothiazepine and dihydropyridine L-type
calcium channels (65% and 63%, respectively) and rat GABAA t-butyl bicycloorthobenzoate (TBOB) chloride channel (62%). M2 also significantly inhibited the rat
benzothiazepine L-type calcium channel (52%), human estrogen receptor (ER) alpha
(64%), and rat GABAA TBOB chloride channel (53%). No significant off-target effects
were observed with M3, M4, or M8.
Safety Pharmacology
Key Findings:
The Applicant conducted four non-GLP safety pharmacology studies using cultured 3D
human hepatocyte spheroids and one non-GLP safety pharmacology study using an in
vitro human liver co-culture microphysiological system (MPS) with the objective of
quantifying the in vitro hepatotoxic potential of tradipitant and its metabolites and to
evaluate their underlying toxicity mechanisms. High variability of the positive controls,
between donors, and between studies was observed. The rationale for the chosen
positive controls was not provided, and the biological and/or clinical context for chosen
endpoints and observed effects was not established. No primary mechanism was
identified by which tradipitant or its metabolites may induce liver injury. In light of the
limitations stated above, the clinical risk of liver injury with tradipitant administration
remains poorly defined; however, these studies support that the risk of drug-induced
liver injury is not an animal-specific finding and is relevant to humans.
PK/ADME/TK
Key Findings:
Tradipitant and its metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were highly bound in vitro using plasma
samples from rat, mouse, and human. Protein binding of M8 was not assessed. There
was no clear effect of concentration on plasma binding of tradipitant or its metabolites in
any species.
Using recombinant human enzymes, UGT1A4 and UGT2B7 were identified as UDPglucuronosyltransferases capable of converting tradipitant to the metabolite M8.
In studies using human liver microsomes, tradipitant competitively inhibited CYP3A
activity with a Ki of 0.92 μM and non-competitively inhibited CYP3A activity with a Ki of
11 μM. Tradipitant competitively inhibited both CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 activity with Ki
values of 9.0 μM and 4.7 μM, respectively. Tradipitant also non-competitively inhibited
CYP2C8 activity with a Ki value of 17 μM. No significant effects were observed on
CYP1A2, CYP2D6, CYP2E1, or CYP2B6 activity.
In a study using primary human hepatocytes, tradipitant was considered a potential
inducer of CYP3A4 activity in vitro. No biologically significant effects on CYP1A2 activity
were observed.

Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Toxicology
Genotoxicity
Key Findings:
Tradipitant metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were negative for genotoxicity in the in vitro
bacterial reverse mutation assay and the in vitro mammalian chromosome aberration
assay using human lymphocytes. M8 was not tested for genotoxic potential.
Special Toxicology Studies
Key Findings:
The Applicant conducted two studies using a “four-organ” MPS comprised of primary
human hepatocytes, induced pluripotent stem cell (iPSC)-derived cardiomyocytes,
primary human renal proximal tubular epithelial cells, and iPSC-derived cortical neurons
with the objective of evaluating the safety of tradipitant. Although the Applicant
positioned these studies as pivotal to determining the safety of tradipitant, these studies
were not conducted in compliance with US FDA GLP regulations; given the absence of
adequate details regarding deviations from GLP and the potential impact of any
deviations on the study validity, this was considered a limitation of these studies. Other
limitations were identified that precluded use of these studies to support regulatory
decision-making in this application, including the narrow scope of toxicity evaluations,
unjustified biological relevance of the system and chosen endpoints, use of a single
donor/cell source per organ, and short duration of the studies (7 or 14 days). Ultimately,
due to these limitations, these studies do not substantively contribute to the safety
profile of tradipitant.
Other Toxicology Studies
Key Findings:
An in silico analysis of tradipitant and metabolites M2, M3, M4, and M8 using various
tools and databases was conducted in 2015 and submitted to the current NDA.
Potential toxicities were identified; however, in light of the completed in vitro and in vivo
toxicity studies with tradipitant and/or its metabolites, the findings in this report are not
considered toxicologically relevant.
Updated in silico analyses were conducted by the Applicant to support the safety of
metabolites M2, M3, and M4 for tradipitant for the prevention of vomiting induced by
motion. These analyses were intended to assess if and how structural differences in the
metabolites might affect their toxicity profile compared to tradipitant. Briefly, these in
silico analyses were conducted using a machine learning-assisted workflow developed
by xAI's Grok system, an advanced large language model trained on scientific datasets
and further validated via the Applicant’s in-house Python scripts. Notably, the
Applicant’s comparative structural prediction data were inferred based on RDKitcomputed descriptors and qualitative thresholds aligned with best practices from
consensus classifiers, rather than direct execution of these models. It is not clear why
the Applicant chose to design a workflow using a large language model and a custom
set of molecular descriptors and use information from existing classifier techniques to
‘infer’ predictions, rather than directly using existing (Q)SAR modeling software.
Although insufficient detail was provided on the consensus classifiers used in the
analyses to fully define the approach, enough information was provided to facilitate
8
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

review of the findings. Overall, the updated in silico analyses predicted that metabolites
M2, M3, and M4 were unlikely to present additional toxicity concerns compared with
tradipitant.
Nonclinical Assessment and Recommendation
The NDA submission included reports of 24 new nonclinical studies that had not been
(b) (4) These
previously submitted to IND 141315 or cross-referenced from IND
included reports of primary pharmacology, safety pharmacology, pharmacokinetics,
genetic toxicology, special toxicology, and other toxicology studies with tradipitant
and/or its metabolites. In addition to review of the individual studies, this document
contains an assessment of the available nonclinical data with tradipitant with regard to
two major issues:
1. If the available nonclinical information, including additional in vitro assays,
adequately support the safety of unrestricted use of tradipitant over a lifetime for
the prevention of vomiting induced by motion; and
2. How the available nonclinical information addresses the safety of the four major
human metabolites of tradipitant, M2, M3, M4, and M8.
Adequacy of Nonclinical Data to Support the Safety of Unrestricted Use
As previously documented, the Applicant has not conducted a chronic toxicity study in a
nonrodent species. The available nonclinical data with tradipitant, including repeat-dose
toxicity studies of up to 6 months in rats and up to 3 months in dogs, do not support the
safety of unrestricted, repeated intermittent use of tradipitant over a lifetime for the
prevention of vomiting induced by motion. While the duration of the nonclinical toxicity
studies supports the safety of tradipitant itself for repeat intermittent dosing for a limited
duration, it does not support unrestricted use over a lifetime.
As an alternative to conducting a chronic toxicity study in a nonrodent species, the
Applicant submitted a total of seven additional in vitro assays to support the long-term
safety of tradipitant.
Four studies using cultured 3D human hepatocyte spheroids and one study using an in
vitro human liver co-culture MPS were conducted with tradipitant and/or its major
metabolites with the objective of validating/quantifying the drug’s hepatotoxic potential
and to evaluate its underlying toxicity mechanisms. Treatment with tradipitant and all
four major metabolites resulted in decreased hepatocyte viability that was deemed
biologically relevant by the Applicant. Despite their limitations, these studies support the
risk for tradipitant to cause liver injury, initially identified in Han Wistar rats, and that the
potential for liver injury is not animal-specific, as these studies used human cells.
It should be noted that, while adaptive liver changes were observed in all animal toxicity
studies with tradipitant and adverse liver findings were only observed in the 3-month
Han Wister rat study, the metabolites M2, M4, and M8 were not present in the rat
studies. Therefore, the toxicological impact of these compounds, including the potential
for hepatoxicity, has not been adequately elucidated in animal studies.

9
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Two additional in vitro studies using a “four-organ” MPS comprised of human
hepatocytes, cardiomyocytes, renal proximal tubule epithelial cells, and cortical neurons
were conducted to evaluate the safety of tradipitant. Limitations in the design and
conduct of the studies were identified, including the narrow scope of toxicity evaluations,
unjustified biological relevance of the system and chosen endpoints, lack of compliance
with US FDA GLP regulations, and short duration of treatment (up to 14 days) relative to
the intended clinical use.
Ultimately, the submitted alternative in vitro assays do not substantially contribute to the
safety profile of tradipitant, and a chronic toxicity study in a nonrodent species is still
scientifically warranted to support unrestricted use over a lifetime. Moreover, the in vitro
liver safety pharmacology studies support a human-relevant risk of hepatotoxicity with
tradipitant use that was initially identified in the animal studies, though the clinical risk of
liver injury with tradipitant administration remains poorly defined.
Overall, there continues to be insufficient nonclinical safety information to support the
safety of unrestricted, repeated intermittent use of tradipitant over a lifetime in humans,
and this is an approvability issue for the current application.
Nonclinical Qualification of Major Human Metabolites
The Applicant has identified four major metabolites of tradipitant in human plasma: M2,
M3, M4, and M8. These metabolites have been investigated in a series of nonclinical
studies, and their plasma levels were measured in select toxicity studies conducted in
rats and mice. Metabolite levels were not measured in any toxicity study conducted in
dogs. Based on available data, these metabolites are considered to be
pharmacologically active, toxicologically relevant, and present at significant levels
compared to the parent drug, tradipitant. These factors support that the safety of levels
of these metabolites should be qualified based on nonclinical data. Among these
metabolites, only M3 was present in nonclinical studies in sufficient levels to be qualified
for safety; M2, M4, and M8 are disproportionate human metabolites, and their safety
was not adequately addressed with in vivo animal studies.
The Applicant proposed, and the review team considered, a Weight of Evidence (WoE)
analysis to support that metabolites M2, M4, and M8 were not expected to be
toxicologically different from tradipitant itself, thereby allowing for qualification based on
total tradipitant drug-related exposure rather than on an individual basis. (Q)SAR
analyses of the di-aryl alcohol and/or N-oxide moieties present on M2, M3, and M4
compared to tradipitant were conducted by FDA/CDER, and comparative computational
prediction analyses were conducted in parallel by the Applicant. These analyses
supported that the structural differences would not result in new or exaggerated
toxicities with the metabolites compared to tradipitant itself. Analysis of the recently
provided in vitro pharmacology, pharmacokinetic, and genotoxicity data (reviewed in this
document) further supported the toxicological similarity of tradipitant and all four major
metabolites. Therefore, the WoE supported that qualification of the disproportionate
human metabolites (M2, M4, and M8) using total drug-related exposure, rather than on
an individual basis, was scientifically appropriate.
10
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Comparison of the total tradipitant drug-related exposure (i.e., tradipitant plus all
measured active metabolites) between animals and humans demonstrated sufficient
exposure margins such that metabolites M2, M4, and M8 were considered qualified
based on the available nonclinical data. Thus, there is no safety concern with the levels
of the major human metabolites.
Notably, it is not generally expected that such a WoE approach will be appropriate for
the qualification of metabolites in other development programs, as each situation must
be evaluated on its own merits based on the totality of data and information. Although in
silico analyses are highly valuable in certain situations, they are unlikely to routinely
address safety concerns in lieu of typical in vivo toxicity studies.

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

1 Background
1.1 Regulatory History
NDA 220152 for tradipitant for the acute prevention of vomiting induced by motion in
adults was submitted on December 30, 2024 (SDN 1). This submission included 20
reports of nonclinical studies that had not been previously submitted to IND 141315 or
cross-referenced from other INDs with tradipitant. Following the nonclinical filing review,
additional information requests were communicated in the Filing Review Issues
Identified Letter.1 In response, the Applicant submitted three additional reports of
nonclinical studies (SDN 9: March 25, 2025). Finally, following the Applicant’s
suggestion that the levels of the major human metabolites of tradipitant were qualified
for safety based on their structural similarity and thus toxicological similarity to
tradipitant (SDN 22: September 19, 2025) and resulting information requests,2,3 the
Applicant submitted updated in silico analyses of the metabolites to support their safety
in lieu of additional nonclinical studies (SDNs 23 and 24: October 15 and 22, 2025).
This document contains reviews of these 24 new studies as well as a detailed scientific
discussion of the nonclinical review issues relevant to this application.

4 Pharmacology
4.1 Primary Pharmacology
Radioligand Binding of Tradipitant and Its Metabolites M2, M3, and M4
Tradipitant: ab28300-1190604
M2: ab28300-1190601
M3: ab28300-1190602
M4: ab28300-1190603
The radioligand binding of tradipitant (named VLY-686 in this report) and its metabolites
M2, M3, and M4 to a panel of 189 receptors, channels, and transporters was evaluated
using rodent tissues or recombinant expression of human proteins in cell lines, as
appropriate. As only a single concentration (10 µM) of each test article was used, IC50
values were not calculated. Inhibition of > 50% was considered biologically significant.
Tradipitant significantly inhibited the rat benzothiazepine and dihydropyridine L-type
calcium channels (65% and 63%, respectively), rat GABAA t-butyl bicyclo-orthobenzoate
(TBOB) chloride channel (62%), and human tachykinin NK-1 receptor (100%).
Metabolite M2 significantly inhibited the rat benzothiazepine L-type calcium channel
(52%), human estrogen receptor (ER) alpha (64%), rat GABAA TBOB chloride channel
(53%), and human tachykinin NK-1 receptor (100%). Metabolites M3 and M4
significantly inhibited the human tachykinin NK-1 receptor (100%), with no significant
inhibitory effects observed on other targets.

In Vitro Pharmacology Study of VLY-686 and VLY-686 M8
FR095-0045286 (aka 100071541)
The radioligand binding of tradipitant (named VLY-686 in this report) and metabolite M8
to a panel of 43 receptors, channels, and transporters was evaluated using rodent
tissues or recombinant expression of human proteins in cell lines, as appropriate.
Inhibition of >50% was considered biologically significant.
Both tradipitant and M8 inhibited the NK-1 receptor ≥99.0% at the highest tested
concentration of 10 µM. M8 was approximately 3 times more potent at the NK-1
receptor compared to tradipitant (IC50 values of 0.49 and 3.3 nM, respectively).

4.3 Safety Pharmacology Studies
Reviewer Note: The safety pharmacology study reviewed below were not conducted in
accordance with US FDA GLP regulations. Despite that limitation, evidence of
hepatoxicity was identified, as described below.
4.3.1 Mechanistic analysis of tradipitant in vitro hepatotoxicity in
spheroid model

(b) (4)

3D

HPTOX-3550
Methods:
As stated in the study report, the objective of this non-GLP study4 was to quantify the in
vitro hepatotoxic potential of tradipitant and to evaluate its underlying toxicity
mechanisms. Tradipitant was evaluated using 3D spheroid cultures of primary human
hepatocytes as previously reported by Bell et al.5 In each experiment, primary human
hepatocytes from a single human donor were seeded into ultra-low attachment plates
and cultured for seven days until spontaneous self-aggregation was observed. Cells
were then cultured overnight in serum free medium prior to treatment with tradipitant
(Lot # DVVE18001M6) at approximately 3.5, 7.0, 10.5, 17.5, or 35 µg/mL (i.e.,
approximately 10, 20, 30, 50, or 100x the Applicant’s reported clinical Cmax of 350
ng/mL).
Reviewer note: Steady state PK data for subjects who experience vomiting induced by
motion are derived from Trial VP-VLY-686-1102, in which healthy volunteers were
administered tradipitant at 85 mg twice daily (BID) for 12 days (assumed but not
(b) (4)

17
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

confirmed to represent steady state) under fasted conditions. In this Trial, the Cmax for
tradipitant was reported as 327 ng/mL. Thus, the concentrations used in this study
represent tradipitant exposure approximately 1.1-, 3.2-, 11-, 21-, 32-, or 107-fold the
Cmax, respectively, in this population. It should be noted that the Applicant did not
conduct in vitro to in vivo extrapolation (IVIVE) analyses to determine how the in vitro
concentration relates to that observed in humans. In fact, a direct comparison of Cmax
and in vitro concentrations is scientifically inappropriate as such an “approach
oversimplifies the complex relationship between in vitro and in vivo drug behavior and
can lead to inaccurate conclusions.”7 Thus, the stated effect margins should be
interpreted with caution, and all observed effects are discussed in terms of absolute
concentration.
DMSO was used as the negative control in all experiments. Drug-containing medium
was replaced every 48-72 hours for a total of 2 or 4 weeks.
A total of five donors were used in this study for five individual experimental cultures;
the cells from each donor were not pooled. Donors included three females and two
males ranging in age from 27-50 years old, of varying ethnicity, who died of non-liverrelated causes, and were not reported to be on any medication at the time of death. At
least eight spheroids per donor per endpoint were evaluated.
Table 3. Characteristics of Human Hepatocyte Donors in Study HPTOX-3550

Table copied directly from study report (Study HPTOX-3550, Appendix).

Endpoints tested in this study included cell viability as determined via quantification of
ATP using a commercial kit; cholestatic potential as determined via ATP quantification
following co-culture of spheroids with a physiological, non-toxic mixture of bile acids per
Hendriks et al8; effects of inflammation on cell viability following co-culture of spheroids
7 Albrecht, W. Which concentrations are optimal for in vitro testing? Excli j, 2020. 19: p. 1172-1173.
8 Hendriks, D., Fredriksson Puigvert, L., Messner, S. et al. Hepatic 3D spheroid models for the detection

and study of compounds with cholestatic liability. Sci Rep 6, 35434 (2016).

Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

with 10 ng/ml each of pro-inflammatory cytokines tumor necrosis factor alpha (TNFα),
interferon gamma (INFγ), and interleukin (IL)-6; generation of reactive oxygen species
(ROS) using a commercial kit; and effects on intracellular triglyceride levels using
staining with a commercial dye and measurement of fluorescence at 572 nm.
Tradipitant was considered hepatotoxic if viability was reduced to < 80% of control
levels in experiments measuring cell viability.9
Results:
Although notable inter-donor variability was observed, cell viability generally decreased
in spheroids treated with tradipitant in a time- and concentration-dependent manner.
Toxicity was observed in Donors 1 and 2, with cell viability reduced to ≈80% of controls
at ≥ 7.0 µg/mL following 4 weeks of tradipitant exposure, and to < 20% of controls at 35
µg/mL following 2 or 4 weeks of treatment. Treatment with the positive control,
chlorpromazine, induced limited or moderate toxicity in these donors (viability ≈60%90% of controls). In Donors 3 and 5, toxicity (viability ≈60%-80% of controls) was
observed at ≥ 7.0 and ≥ 17.5 µg/mL, respectively, after 2 weeks of treatment; however,
no biologically significant toxicity was observed following 4 weeks of treatment except at
the highest concentration of 35 µg/mL (viability ≈60% of controls). Toxicity in Donor 4
was observed to be the most clearly dose- and time-dependent, with biologically
significant decreases in cell viability observed following treatment at ≥ 10.5 µg/mL for 2
weeks and ≥ 17.5 µg/mL for 4 weeks.
Decreased cell viability was observed at 7.0 µg/mL in 3/5 donors. However, when
combining the results for all 5 donors, biologically significant decreases in cell viability
were observed at ≥ 17.5 µg/mL following 4 weeks of treatment.

The cholestatic potential of tradipitant in the hepatocyte spheroids was determined via
the cholestatic index (Cix), which is calculated by dividing the viability of spheroids cotreated with bile acids by the viability of spheroids treated with compound only.
Compounds are assumed to induce cholestasis if the Cix is ≤ 0.8 (see Table 4).
Decreased cell viability resulting from the induction of cholestasis in the hepatocyte
spheroids (Cix ≤ 0.8; i.e., tradipitant toxicity was amplified by the physiological bile acid
mixture at these concentrations) was observed in four donors at 35 µg/mL for 2 weeks
and in Donors 3 and 5 only at ≥ 17.5 µg/mL for 4 weeks. The experiment with Donor 4
failed for technical reasons.

The Applicant investigated the synergistic effects of tradipitant and pro-inflammatory
cytokines (TNFα, INFγ, and IL-6) on cell viability.
Reviewer note: Based on the Applicant’s presentation of data in this section and the
lack of raw data provided, it is difficult to distinguish between effects that are additive (or
sub-additive) and synergistic. As the cytotoxic effects of the cytokine mixture alone were
strong, the overall nature of the combined effect may be obscured under the tested
conditions. Additionally, the expected effects of control compounds nimesulide and
buspirone were not defined.
The combination of cytokines alone was strongly cytotoxic, decreasing viability by 58%
and 90% after 2 weeks and 4 weeks, respectively. Again, the experiment with donor 4
failed for technical reasons. In the other 4 donors, treatment with tradipitant at all tested
concentrations (≥ 3.5 µg/mL) for 2 or 4 weeks resulted in larger decreases in cell
viability compared to the cytokines alone. Based on these findings, inflammation may
increase the risk of livery injury with tradipitant.

4.3.2 Analysis of the interaction between inflammatory signaling and tradipitant in vitro
(b) (4) 3D spheroid model
hepatotoxicity in
HPTOX-3560
Methods:
As stated in the study report, the objective of this non-GLP study4 was to quantify the in
vitro hepatotoxic potential of tradipitant and to evaluate its underlying toxicity
mechanisms. Tradipitant was evaluated using 3D spheroid cultures of primary human
hepatocytes as previously reported by Bell et al.5 In each experiment, primary human
hepatocytes from a single human donor were seeded into ultra-low attachment plates
and cultured for 7 days until spontaneous self-aggregation was observed. Cells were
then cultured overnight in serum free medium prior to treatment with tradipitant (Lot #
DVVE18001M6) at approximately 0.35, 1.05, 3.5, 7.0, 10.5, 17.5, or 35 µg/mL (i.e.,
approximately 1, 3, 10, 20, 30, 50, or 100x the Applicant’s reported clinical Cmax of 350
ng/mL); this concentration range was selected by the Applicant based on the findings in
Study HPTOX-3550.
Reviewer note: As stated previously, the concentrations used in this study represent
tradipitant exposure at approximately 11-, 21-, 32-, 54-, or 107-fold the clinical Cmax of
327 ng/mL, respectively, as determined in healthy volunteers following 12 days of
25
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

dosing at 85 mg BID. The Applicant did not conduct IVIVE analyses to determine how
the in vitro concentration relates to that observed in humans. In fact, a direct
comparison of Cmax and in vitro concentrations is scientifically inappropriate as such an
“approach oversimplifies the complex relationship between in vitro and in vivo drug
behavior and can lead to inaccurate conclusions.”7 Thus, the stated effect margins
should be interpreted with caution, and all observed effects are discussed in terms of
absolute concentration.
DMSO was used as the negative control in all experiments. Drug-containing medium
was replaced every 48-72 hours for a total of 2 or 4 weeks.
A total of five donors were used in this study; the cells from each donor were not
pooled. Demographic and health information were not provided for the donors. Eight
spheroids per donor per endpoint were evaluated.
Cell viability was determined via quantification of ATP using a commercial kit following
treatment with tradipitant alone and following co-culture of spheroids with proinflammatory cytokines (TNFα, INFγ, and IL-6; each individually and in combination at
0.1, 1, and 10 ng/mL). Treatments were considered hepatotoxic if cell viability was
reduced to < 80% of control levels.9
Results:
Reviewer note: The Applicant’s interpretation of these results does not clearly report or
identify effects that are synergistic versus those that are additive or sub-additive. As no
raw data were provided and the presentation of the data did not allow for potential
synergistic effects to be directly determined, the results reported here should be
interpreted with caution. Synergistic effects were identified by the reviewer as those
being greater or equal to 1.1 times the estimated additive effect (i.e., the sum of the
tradipitant only effect and the cytokines only effect, as estimated from the bar graph).
Synergistic effects were only analyzed for the cytokine mixtures, not for the individual
cytokines, due to the large number of conditions tested. Notably, the Applicant did not
provide any rationale to explain how the cytokine conditions are relevant to the
indication of vomiting induced by motion, as these subjects are generally considered
healthy in the absence of the motion stimulus.
Summary of Findings
Tradipitant induced hepatotoxicity in 4/5 donors after 2 or 4 weeks of treatment,
evidenced by decreased cell viability of the hepatocyte spheroids compared to controls.
The concentration(s) at which biologically significant toxicity occurred were highly
donor-dependent.
Overall, no clear synergistic effects of tradipitant with pro-inflammatory cytokines (TNFα,
INFγ, IL-6, or the combination) were observed on the viability of hepatocyte spheroids.
However, interpretation of these data was severely limited by the lack of raw data
provided, the Applicant’s method of analysis, the high variability of data generated for

26
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

each individual donor, and the frequently large effects observed with tradipitant or
cytokines alone.
Reviewer note: In this study, treatment with tradipitant at 3.5 µg/mL (or less) for 2 or 4
weeks resulted in biologically significant decreases in cell viability in 4/5 donors. This is
a lower concentration than that observed in Study HPTOX-3550 (7.0 µg/mL). A possible
explanation for the increased sensitivity could not be determined, and no explanation
was provided.
Individual Donor Findings
For Donor 1, biologically significant, dose-dependent hepatotoxicity was observed at
tradipitant concentrations ≥ 3.5 µg/mL following treatment for both 2 and 4 weeks.
None of the cytokines (TNFα, INFγ, IL-6) alone significantly impacted cell viability;
however, treatment with the cytokine mixture resulted in decreased cell viability at 10
ng/mL at 2 weeks and at ≥ 1 ng/mL at 4 weeks.
No combined effects of tradipitant and individual cytokines were reported for this donor.
Sporadic instances of synergistic effects of tradipitant and the cytokine mixture on cell
viability were identified in this review, but no consistent or concentration-dependent
synergy was observed in this donor following 2 weeks of treatment. Synergistic effects
were identified with tradipitant at 0.35 µg/mL only using all concentrations of the
cytokine mixture following 4 weeks of treatment, but not with other combinations.

4.3.3 In vitro hepatotoxicity assessment of compounds in
model

(b) (4)

3D spheroid

HPTOX-3540
Methods:
As stated in the study report, the objective of this non-GLP study4 was to quantify the in
vitro hepatotoxic potential of tradipitant and to evaluate its underlying toxicity
mechanisms. Tradipitant was evaluated using 3D spheroid cultures of primary human
hepatocytes as previously reported by Bell et al.5 In each experiment, primary human
hepatocytes from a single human donor were seeded into ultra-low attachment plates
and cultured for 7 days until spontaneous self-aggregation was observed. Cells were
then cultured overnight in serum free medium prior to treatment with tradipitant (Lot #
DVVE18001M6) at approximately 1, 5, 20, or 50x the Applicant’s reported clinical Cmax
of 350 ng/mL (i.e., approximately 0.35, 1.75, 7.0, or 17.5 µg/mL).
Reviewer note: As stated previously, the concentrations used in this study represent
tradipitant exposure at approximately 1.1-, 5.4-, 21-, or 54-fold the clinical Cmax of 327
ng/mL, respectively, as determined in healthy volunteers following 12 days of dosing at
85 mg BID. The Applicant did not conduct IVIVE analyses to determine how the in vitro
concentration relates to that observed in humans. In fact, a direct comparison of Cmax
and in vitro concentrations is scientifically inappropriate as such an “approach
oversimplifies the complex relationship between in vitro and in vivo drug behavior and
can lead to inaccurate conclusions.”7 Thus, the stated effect margins should be
interpreted with caution, and all observed effects are discussed in terms of absolute
concentration.
DMSO was used as the negative control in all experiments. Positive controls for
different mechanisms of hepatotoxicity included fialuridine (1x Cmax = 0.5 µM),
chlorpromazine (0.84 µM), bosentan (7.43 µM), acetaminophen (136 µM), and
troglitazone (6.08 µM); however, the mechanisms and expected effects of the various
positive controls were not defined. Drug-containing medium was replaced every 48-72
hours for a total of 2 or 4 weeks.
A total of three donors were used in this study. Donors included two males and one
female, ranging from 25-28 years of age, of varying ethnicity, negative for hepatitis virus
infections and HIV, who died of non-liver-related causes, and were not reported to be on
any medication at the time of death. An average of 37±18 spheroids per donor per
endpoint were evaluated.

molecular signatures of aprepitant and serlopitant exposures in 3D primary human liver
spheroids with or without co-exposure to cytokines.
All experiments in this study used 3D spheroid cultures of primary human hepatocytes
as previously reported by Bell et al.14 Briefly, primary human hepatocytes from a single
human donor were seeded into ultra-low attachment plates and cultured for at least
seven days until spontaneous self-aggregation was observed. Cells were then cultured
overnight in serum free medium prior to treatment.
In part one of the study, spheroids were treated with tradipitant at approximately 1, 3,
10, 20, 30, or 100x the Applicant’s reported clinical Cmax of 350 ng/mL (i.e., 0.35, 1.05,
3.5, 7.05, 10.5, or 35 μg/mL). Spheroids were alternatively treated with metabolites M2,
M3, M4, or M8 at the same concentrations (although these concentrations are not
considered to reflect the corresponding clinical Cmax values of the individual
metabolites). Spheroids were also treated with tradipitant or metabolites in combination
with a cytokine cocktail containing TNFα, INFγ, or IL-6 at 1 ng/mL each.
Reviewer note: As stated previously, the concentrations of tradipitant used in this study
represent tradipitant exposure at approximately 1.1-, 3.2-, 11-, 21-, 32-, or 107-fold the
clinical Cmax of 327 ng/mL, respectively, as determined in healthy volunteers following
12 days of dosing at 85 mg BID. The concentrations of metabolites M2, M3, and M4
used in this study represent approximately 3.4- to 343-, 3.6- to 358-, and 3.4- to 337fold, respectively, the individual clinical Cmax values of the metabolites; a comparison to
M8 cannot be made, as no steady state parameters are available for that metabolite.
The Applicant did not conduct IVIVE analyses to determine how the in vitro
concentration relates to that observed in humans. In fact, a direct comparison of Cmax
and in vitro concentrations is scientifically inappropriate as such an “approach
oversimplifies the complex relationship between in vitro and in vivo drug behavior and
can lead to inaccurate conclusions.”7 Thus, the stated effect margins should be
interpreted with caution, and all observed effects are discussed in terms of absolute
concentration.
DMSO was used as the negative control in all experiments. Drug-containing medium
was replaced every 48-72 hours for a total of 14 days.
A total of four donors were used in this study for four individual experimental cultures;
the cells from each donor were not pooled. Donors included one male and three
females ranging in age from 25-42 years old, of varying ethnicity, who died of non-liverrelated causes, and were not reported to be on any medication at the time of death.

Cell viability was measured via quantification of ATP using the CellTiter-Glo

or 6 μg/mL in the presence or absence of the cytokine cocktail. Aprepitant and
serlopitant concentrations were chosen based on a previous study. Total RNA was
isolated and stored at -80°C until further processing. See study report for further details
on RNA sequencing and data analysis methods. At the time of NDA submission, the
results of this analysis were pending and have not been submitted. However, the lack of
data from part two of the study is not considered to impact the review of the safety of
tradipitant and its metabolites for this application.
Results:
Reviewer note: This study investigated the effects of tradipitant and its metabolites on
hepatocyte viability in both the presence and absence of a defined cytokine cocktail.
The Applicant did not provide any rationale to explain how the cytokine conditions are
relevant to the indication of vomiting induced by motion, and these subjects are
generally considered healthy in the absence of the motion stimulus. Thus, this review
will focus on the effects of tradipitant and its metabolites alone, in the absence of
cytokines. To the extent that results could be interpreted given the lack of raw data and
normalization of the cytokine data in the study report, it should be noted that the
combined effects of tradipitant or its metabolites and the cytokine cocktail were
generally additive or sub-additive, consistent with the findings previously reported in
Study HPTOX-3560 (see Section 4.3.2).

Summary of Findings
Overall, biologically significant decreases in viability (i.e., viability < 80% of control) were
observed with tradipitant and all tested metabolites. The concentration(s) at which
biologically significant toxicity occurred and the maximum effect were highly donordependent.
Reviewer Note: Following two weeks of treatment with tradipitant, biologically significant
decreases in cell viability in this study were observed at a notably lower concentration
for 2/3 donors (0.350 versus 17.5 µg/mL) and at a higher concentration for the
remaining donor (35 versus 7.0 µg/mL) compared to that observed in Study HPTOX3550. A possible explanation for the increased sensitivity could not be determined, and
no explanation was provided.
Treatment with tradipitant resulted in biologically significant decreases in viability in all
donors at concentrations as low as 0.35 μg/mL (the lowest concentration tested,
approximately 1.1-fold the clinical Cmax of 327 ng/mL in healthy volunteers following
administration at 85 mg BID for 12 days16). Decreased viability with tradipitant was
generally not concentration-dependent. The maximum decrease in viability was
observed at 10.5-35 µM and ranged from 50%-90%.
Treatment with M2 resulted in biologically significant decreases in viability in all donors
at concentrations as low as 0.35 μg/mL (the lowest concentration tested). Decreased
viability with M2 was generally concentration-dependent, though the incremental
increases at higher concentrations tended to be small or absent. The maximum
decrease in viability was observed at 7.05-10.5 μg/mL and ranged from 35%-90%.
Treatment with M3 resulted in biologically significant decreases in viability in all donors
at concentrations as low as 1.05 μg/mL but more commonly at 10.5 or 35 μg/mL. The
maximum decrease in viability was observed at 1.05 μg/mL in Donor 3 but at 35 μg/mL
in other donors and ranged from 60%-100%.
Treatment with M4 resulted in biologically significant decreases in viability in all donors
at concentrations as low as 0.35 μg/mL (the lowest concentration tested). Decreased
viability with M2 was generally not concentration-dependent. The maximum decrease in
viability was most often observed at 3.5 μg/mL and ranged from 30%-70%.
Treatment with M8 resulted in biologically significant decreases in viability in only two of
the four tested donors. In those two donors, decreased viability was observed at
concentrations as low as 10.5 and 0.35 μg/mL, respectively. The maximum decrease in
viability was observed at 7.05-10.5 μg/mL and ranged from 30%-80%.
16 Based on data from Study VP-VLY-686-1102. Following 12 days of dosing at 85 mg BID in healthy

volunteers, Cmax = 327 ng/mL. Although the study was inconclusive as to whether steady state was
achieved, steady state was achieved within 7 days of daily dosing in healthy male subjects in Study H8REW-HJAD. As subjects with vomiting induced by motion are considered otherwise healthy in the absence
of a motion stimulus, and in the absence of robust multi-dose PK from Study VP-VLY-686-3403 for this
indication, PK from healthy volunteers was deemed the most appropriate for this evaluation.

Individual Donor Findings
For Donor 1, biologically significant, concentration-dependent hepatotoxicity was
observed with tradipitant at all tested concentrations (≥ 0.35 μg/mL) following treatment
for two weeks, with a maximum decrease in viability of approximately 50% compared to
DMSO control. Treatment with M2 resulted in decreases in viability at all tested
concentrations that were generally concentration-dependent, with a maximum decrease
in viability of approximately 70%. Toxicity with M3 was observed primarily at 10.5 and
35 μg/mL, with a maximum decrease of 85%. Toxicity with M4 was observed at 3.5
μg/mL and higher, with a maximum decrease in viability of 65%. Finally, toxicity was
observed with M8 at 10.5 and 35 μg/mL, with a maximum decrease in viability of 30%.

4.3.5 Evaluation of Toxicity of Tradipitant in the Human Co-Culture Liver-Chip
SRV-0028
Methods:
As stated in the submitted report, this non-GLP study was intended to “validate the preclinical toxicology safety profiles of tradipitant and its metabolites in the human Liverchip.” and “to compare the toxicity profile of tradipitant to other known liver toxicants that
have been approved for clinical use.” To do so, tradipitant was evaluated using
(b) (4) Human Co-Culture Liver-Chip, which consists of primary human hepatocytes
and liver sinusoid endothelial cells (LSECs). Organ-chips were composed of
polydimethylsiloxane (PDMS). Primary human hepatocytes were cultured in the top
channel on an extracellular matrix (ECM)-coated membrane with an overlay of matrix to
form an “ECM sandwich.” Human LSECs were seeded on the opposite side of the same
membrane in the bottom channel. After 3 days in static culture, the chips were
connected to the organ-chip culture instrument and cultured under constant flow until
(b) (4) Liver-Chip Co-Culture
the start of compound addition, in accordance with
Protocol. Starting at 72 hours after connection, all cells were incubated with the
appropriate compound solution under flow at 150 µL/h for 2 days, followed by 30 µL/h
for the remainder of the study (see Results for more information on flow rate).
Chips were continuously treated for up to 168 hours (7 days) with tradipitant (Lot # RSIN-VDDE170226) at 1.3 μM (772.4 ng/mL), 7.6 μM (4,480 ng/mL), or 26.3 μM (15,448
ng/mL). According to the Applicant, these concentrations are equivalent to
approximately 3x, 20x, and 68x the Cmax of 85 mg tradipitant administered twice daily
(BID) in humans at steady state, respectively, as determined in clinical study VP-VLY686-1102.
Reviewer note: In the provided rationale for the selected tradipitant concentrations, the
Applicant did not explicitly state the referenced clinical Cmax. Based on the numbers
provided, the value used was approximately 230 ng/mL, although it was inconsistent
between the three concentrations/Cmax multiples provided (calculated values of 257,
224, and 227 ng/mL, respectively), potentially due to rounding and/or lack of significant
digits. As stated previously, a clinical Cmax of 327 ng/mL was determined in healthy
volunteers following 12 days of dosing at 85 mg BID. Based on this value, the
concentrations used in this study represent tradipitant exposure at approximately 2.4-,
14-, or 47-fold the clinical Cmax in the context of the current indication. The Applicant did
not conduct IVIVE analyses to determine how the in vitro concentration relates to that
observed in humans. In fact, a direct comparison of Cmax and in vitro concentrations is
scientifically inappropriate as such an “approach oversimplifies the complex relationship
between in vitro and in vivo drug behavior and can lead to inaccurate conclusions.”7
Thus, the stated effect margins should be interpreted with caution, and all observed
effects are discussed in terms of absolute concentration.

49
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Metabolite M2 was tested at 0.12 and 2.43 µM, M3 at 0.11 and 2.29 µM, M4 at 0.12 and
2.48 µM, and M8 at 0.05 and 0.90 µM; rationale for these concentrations was not
provided. Positive controls for different mechanisms of hepatotoxicity included
acetaminophen (10 mM), fialuridine (30 µM), benzbromarone (100µM), and bosentan
(30 µM). Rationale for the selected positive controls or their expected effects in this
system were not provided. DMSO was used as the vehicle control in all experiments.
A total of three donors were used in this study. Donors included two males and one
female, ranging from 26-77 years of age, who did not smoke or drink alcohol, and who
died of non-liver-related or unknown causes. Generally, six chips were used for each
vehicle control condition, while four to five chips were used for each positive control,
tradipitant, or metabolite condition.
Table 8. Characteristics of Human Hepatocyte Donors in Study SRV-0028

Table copied directly from study report (Study SRV-0028, Table 3).

Tradipitant and its metabolites were measured by LCMS. Evaluated endpoints included
morphology, albumin, alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase
(AST), ROS generation, caspase-cleaved cytokeratin 18 (CCK-18) expression, and
miR-122 expression. Albumin, ALT, AST, ROS, and CCK-18 were measured via
commercial ELISA kits (with adjustment for cell number and dilution). miR-122
expression was measured by RT-qPCR. Finally, hepatocytes were stained with
AdipoRed to qualitatively evaluate intracellular lipid droplets, while both hepatocytes
and LSCECs were stained with CellROX to qualitatively evaluate ROS/oxidative stress.
For morphology analyses, four images were obtained per chip per timepoint. Images
were independently analyzed by two individuals, one of whom was blinded to the study.
Morphology was scored qualitatively according to the classification below.

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Figure 19. Hepatocyte and LSEC Morphology Classification Systems

LSEC: Liver sinusoid endothelial cells.
Figures copied directly from study report (Study SRV-0028, pages 25-26).

Results:
During initial test runs, it was determined that 7.62 µM tradipitant applied at a flow rate
of 30 µL/h was highly absorbed in the chip itself, resulting in a significant decrease in
exposure to hepatocytes (approaching zero) and LSECs (as low as 0.5 µM). Increasing
the fluidic flow rate to 150 µL/h improved the uniform distribution of tradipitant and
increased the likelihood that cells would be exposed to the drug (range 1.063-6.384 µM
for hepatocytes and 1.182-6.195 µM for LSECs). Based on these results, the flow rate
was adjusted to 150 µL/h during the treatment phase of the experiment, and an
additional concentration of tradipitant (26.28 µM) was included. Additionally flow
optimization was not reported for the metabolites.

Morphology
Summary of Findings
Reviewer note: Representative images for each donor can be viewed in the study
report. All analyses are qualitative, and the descriptions below are based on the
representative images and heat map summaries provided.
Overall, positive controls induced severe time- and dose-dependent toxicity in both
hepatocytes and LSECs. At 26.28 µM, the highest tested concentration, tradipitant
induced mild toxicity in hepatocytes from Donors 2 and 3 and moderate toxicity in
hepatocytes from Donor 1. Tradipitant did not induce significant toxicity in LSECs at any
tested concentration. Sporadic findings of minimal to mild toxicity were observed in
hepatocytes and LSECs with metabolites M2, M3, M4, and/or M8 (generally only at the
high concentration of each) following 168 hours of treatment.

53
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Pre-Treatment
Prior to treatment, all images from all donors demonstrated healthy hepatocytes and
LSECs, with morphology scores of 0.
6 Hours
Only Donors 2 and 3 were evaluated for morphology at the 6-hour timepoint.
Hepatocytes and LSECs treated with 100 μM benzbromarone both demonstrated mild
toxicity (≈20% dead cells with cells beginning to lose their distinct tight junctions and
granulation of the cytoplasm). No toxicity was observed in Donor 2 with the remaining
controls or in Donor 3 with any positive control after 6 hours.
Hepatocytes and LSECs from both Donors 2 and 3 demonstrated normal, healthy
morphology at all tested concentrations of tradipitant and its metabolites following 6
hours of treatment.
24 Hours
Morphology was evaluated for all donors at the 24-hour timepoint. Mild to moderate
hepatocyte toxicity was induced by 10 mM acetaminophen in all donors, mild toxicity
was induced by 30 μM fialuridine in Donor 1 only, and mild hepatocyte toxicity was
induced by 30 µM bosentan in Donors 1 and 3 when compared to the vehicle control at
24 hours. Treatment with acetaminophen resulted in overall severe granulation of
cytoplasm, loss of junctions in most cells, and dead cells (< 50%). Fialuridine treatment
did not induce significant cell death in hepatocytes, but cells had begun to lose their
distinct cell junctions, and many cells contained granulated cytoplasm. With
benzbromarone, mild to moderate toxicity was observed in hepatocytes and moderate
cell death was observed in LSECs compared to the vehicle control group in all donors at
24 hours.
No morphological changes were observed in hepatocytes or LSECs treated with
tradipitant or its metabolites at any concentration in all donors after 24 hours.
72 Hours
Morphology was evaluated for all donors at the 72-hour timepoint. Hepatocyte toxicity
was observed in at least one donor with all positive controls: moderate to severe toxicity
in all donors with acetaminophen, severe toxicity in Donor 1 only with fialuridine, mild to
moderate toxicity in all donors with bosentan, and severe toxicity in all donors with
benzbromarone compared to the corresponding vehicle control at 72 hours. Mild to
severe toxicity was observed in LSECs from all donors treated with acetaminophen,
mild toxicity was observed in LSECs from Donor 1 only with fialuridine, and moderate to
severe toxicity was observed in LSECs from all donors treated with benzbromarone. No
significant toxicity was observed in LSECs treated with bosentan for 72 hours.
A concentration-dependent increase in hepatotoxicity, including observations of vesicles
akin to steatosis, was observed with tradipitant in all donors compared to vehicle
controls following 72 hours of treatment. No significant morphological changes were

54
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

observed in LSECs treated with tradipitant, nor was toxicity observed in either
hepatocytes or LSECs treated with metabolites at this timepoint.
168 Hours
Following 168 hours of treatment, acetaminophen induced moderate to severe
hepatotoxicity in all donors, fialuridine induced mild (Donors 2 and 3) or severe (Donor
1) toxicity, benzbromarone induced severe toxicity in all donors, and bosentan induced
moderate toxicity in all donors. In LSECs, acetaminophen induced severe toxicity in
Donors 1 and 2, while bosentan induced moderate toxicity observed in all treated
donors at this timepoint. No significant effects were observed on LSECs treated with
fialuridine or benzbromarone.
Low to moderate toxicity was observed in hepatocytes from all donors treated with
tradipitant at 26.28 µM for 168 hours, as indicated by altered hepatocyte morphology
when compared to the vehicle control. Overall severe granulation of cytoplasm was
observed, most of the cells had lost their tight junctions, and < 50% cells appeared
dead. No significant morphological changes were observed in hepatocytes treated with
tradipitant at lower concentrations or in LSECs treated with any concentration of
tradipitant at this timepoint.
In hepatocytes, mild toxicity (≤ 20% dead cells) was observed with M2 at the high
concentration in Donor 2 only. Minimal toxicity was observed with M3 at the high
concentration in Donor 1 only. No significant toxicity was observed in any donor with M4
treatment. Mild toxicity was observed with M8 at both the low and high concentrations in
Donors 2 and 3 only.
In LSECs, minimal toxicity was observed in Donor 1 only with the high concentrations of
M2, M3, and M4. No significant toxicity was observed in LSECs from Donor 1 with M8
or in Donors 2 or 3 with any metabolite at either the low or high concentrations at the
168-hour timepoint.
Immunofluorescence Imaging
AdipoRed staining (intracellular lipid accumulation, hepatocytes only) was conducted in
all donors, while CellROX staining (ROS accumulation, hepatocytes and LSECs) was
conducted in Donors 2 and 3 only. Assessment of immunofluorescent staining of the
hepatocytes from each donor was qualitative only, with representative images provided.
Significant donor-to-donor variations in the lipid accumulation were observed.
Hepatocytes from Donor 1 showed increased lipid accumulation with all positive
controls compared to vehicle. No significant lipid accumulation or changes in ROS
activity were observed in cells from Donors 2 and 3, although these findings may have
been confounded by cell death/loss due to treatment and/or processing in these donors.
A concentration-dependent accumulation of lipids was observed in hepatocytes from
Donor 1 treated with tradipitant compared to vehicle controls. In Donor 2, no significant
lipid accumulation was observed in hepatocytes or LSECs treated with tradipitant, but a
55
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

slight increase in ROS activity was observed in treated compared to control cells. In
Donor 3, no significant lipid accumulation was observed in hepatocytes or LSECs
treated with tradipitant; ROS staining in this donor was minimal under all treatment
conditions.
Lipid accumulation was observed in hepatocytes from Donor 1 with both low and high
concentrations of M2, M3, and M4, and low concentrations of M8 compared to vehicle
controls. In hepatocytes from Donors 2 and 3, no notable lipid accumulation or changes
in ROS activity were observed. No significant effects of the metabolites were observed
in LSECs from any donor.
Biomarker Analysis
Summary of Findings
The Applicant measured albumin, AST, ALT, ROS generation, CCK-18 protein
expression, and miRNA-122 expression in the top channel (hepatocyte) effluent
following 6, 24, 72, and/or 168 hours of treatment. Some factors were also assessed in
cell lysate samples.
• No clear effects of tradipitant or its metabolites were observed on albumin levels.
• No biologically significant effect of tradipitant was observed on AST levels;
however, metabolites M2, M3, M4, and/or M8 had effects on AST that were
potentially biologically significant, although there was high variability between
donors and the general effects were a fraction of those observed with the positive
control, benzbromarone.
• No significant effect of tradipitant was observed on ALT levels; however,
metabolites M2, M3, M4, and/or M8 had effects on ALT that were potentially
biologically significant, with effects approximately 20-50% that of the positive
control, benzbromarone, and similar to that of acetaminophen and bosentan.
• The effects of tradipitant and its metabolites on ROS generation and CCK-18
expression were inconclusive due to a lack of meaningful differences between
the positive and vehicle controls.
• Although increases in miRNA-122 expression were observed with tradipitant
and/or its metabolites, with effects as high as 50-75% of the positive control,
acetaminophen, the biological significance of these changes was not established.
Albumin Levels
Albumin secretion was measured from the top channel (hepatocytes) effluent at 24, 72,
and 168 hours for all donors. An additional timepoint of 6 hours was included for Donors
2 and 3. The Applicant interpreted a decrease in albumin secretion (compared to
vehicle or over time) to indicate a decline in the health of hepatocytes, while an increase
was considered to indicate an improvement in hepatocyte health.
The vehicle control for Donors 1 and 2 demonstrated a decrease in albumin levels at
later timepoints, suggesting an overall decline in hepatocyte health over time.

56
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Treatment with positive control compounds acetaminophen and benzbromarone
consistently resulted in decreased albumin secretion in all three donors compared to
vehicle, although the magnitude and timing of the difference was variable.
While some differences were noted between tradipitant and vehicle control groups, no
clear negative effect of tradipitant or its metabolites was noted on albumin secretion.
Effects were generally minimal, with no concentration-dependence, and were potentially
impacted by declining hepatocyte health.

AST Levels
AST levels were measured from top channel effluent at 24 hours, 72 hours, and 168
hours of treatment for all donors, and an additional 6 hours timepoint was added for
Donors 2 and 3.
Increased AST was consistently observed with benzbromarone for all donors within 72
hours. Increased AST was also observed in Donor 1 with acetaminophen (24 and 72
hours) and fialuridine (168 hours) as well as with bosentan in Donor 3 (24 hours)
compared to vehicle controls.
No consistent effect of tradipitant on AST was noted. In Donor 3 only, increased AST
was observed with tradipitant at 26.28 µM by 24 hours, with levels at 168 hours
reaching approximately 2-fold that of vehicle. Notable changes were not observed with
lower concentrations of tradipitant in Donor 3 or at any concentration in Donors 1 and 2.
Similarly, notable changes in AST levels with tradipitant metabolites were only observed
in Donor 3. Treatment with the low concentration of metabolite M2 or M3 resulted in
increased AST in Donor 3 and 24 and 72 hours (up to 5-fold or more that of the vehicle
control), with differences mostly resolving by 168 hours. Notable changes were not
observed with lower concentrations of metabolites M2 and M3 or any concentration of
M4 or M8 in Donor 3, and no effects of the metabolites were observed in Donors 1 and
2.

ALT Levels
ALT levels were measured from top channel (hepatocyte) effluent at 24, 72, and 168
hours for all donors, and an additional 6 hours timepoint was added for Donors 2 and 3.
The effects of the various positive controls on ALT levels were highly variable between
donors. In Donor 1, significantly increased ALT was observed at ≥ 24 hours with
acetaminophen, fialuridine, and bosentan, while increased ALT was observed at ≥ 72
hours with benzbromarone. In Donor 2, significantly increased ALT was observed at 24
and 72 hours with benzbromarone and at 168 hours with acetaminophen and bosentan.
In Donor 3, significantly increased ALT was observed at 6 and 72 hours with
benzbromarone as well as at 24 hours with bosentan.

59
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

No notable differences in ALT were observed between tradipitant-treated conditions and
the vehicle control in any donor.
The effects of the metabolites on ALT were highly variable between donors. No clear
effect of any metabolite was observed compared to vehicle in Donor 1. For Donor 2,
increased ALT was observed with the low and high concentrations of M2, high
concentration of M3, high concentration of M4, and low and high concentrations of M8;
effects generally peaked at 168 hours. For Donor 3, notable effects on ALT were
observed at 24 hours only with the high concentrations of M2, M3, and M4.

Generation of Reactive Oxygen Species
For Donor 1, ROS generation was measured in the frozen top channel effluent at 24,
72, and 168 hours. However, because no meaningful differences were detected
between the vehicle and positive controls and the half-life of ROS after release is known
to be short, the assay was run at an earlier timepoint and with fresh samples in later
experiments.
Hence, for Donor 2, ROS was measured from the freshly collected effluent following 6,
24, 72, or 168 hours of treatment. These data were also inconclusive, and the effluentbased assay was not run for Donor 3; instead, ROS was assessed by live staining and
imaging (see Immunofluorescence Imaging).

Caspase-Cleaved Cytokeratin 18 Levels
CCK-18 is a protein released from hepatocytes during apoptosis that has been
proposed in the literature as a potential biomarker of liver disease17; this biomarker has
not been clinically validated for this purpose. CCK-18 was measured in the top channel
(hepatocyte) effluent at 24, 72, and 168 for all donors, and an additional timepoint of 6
hours was added for Donors 2 and 3.
The effects of the positive controls on CCK-18 expression were inconsistent between
donors. Increased CCK-18 was observed with acetaminophen at ≥ 24 hours and
fialuridine at 168 hours compared to vehicle in Donor 1, while increased CCK-18 was
observed with benzbromarone at 24 and 72 hours in Donor 3; no notable effects were
observed in Donor 2 at any timepoint.
While some differences were observed between the vehicle control and samples treated
with tradipitant or metabolites, these differences were of small magnitude. Furthermore,
a biologically significant magnitude of change was not defined. Thus, the biological
significance of any changes in CCK-18 expression induced by tradipitant or its
metabolites cannot be determined.

17 Li J, Verhaar AP, Pan Q, de Knegt RJ, Peppelenbosch MP. Serum levels of caspase-cleaved

cytokeratin 18 (CK18-Asp396) predict severity of liver disease in chronic hepatitis B. Clin Exp
Gastroenterol. 2017 Aug 11;10:203-209.

miRNA-122 Expression
miR-122 is a liver-specific miRNA that has been used as a prognostic marker for
patients with hepatocellular carcinoma and has been proposed in the literature as
biomarker for liver injury, including injury induced by acetaminophen18; this biomarker
has not been clinically validated for this purpose. miR-122 was initially measured from
the top channel (hepatocyte) effluent at 24, 72, and 168 hours for all donors, with an
additional timepoint of 6 hours added for Donors 2 and 3.

18 Thakral S, Ghoshal K. miR-122 is a unique molecule with great potential in diagnosis, prognosis of liver

disease, and therapy both as miRNA mimic and antimir. Curr Gene Ther. 2015;15(2):142-50.

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Increased miRNA-122 expression was observed with acetaminophen by 168 hours in
Donors 1 and 2 (< 5-fold increase compared to vehicle). Sporadic effects were observed
with other positive controls.
In Donor 1, an increase in miR-122 expression was observed with the low concentration
of tradipitant at 72 hours only (estimated to be 4-fold that of the vehicle control). No
effects of tradipitant were observed on miR-122 expression in Donor 2. In Donor 3, an
increase in miR-122 expression was observed with the high concentration of tradipitant
at 24hours only (also estimated to be 4-fold that of the vehicle control); this effect was
considered “biologically irrelevant” by the Applicant (no other explanation provided).
Increases in miR-122 expression were observed in Donor 1 with M2, M3, M4, and M8.
No notable increases in miR-122 expression were observed with metabolites in Donor
2. Increased miR-122 expression was also observed with the low concentration of M3 in
Donor 3.

5 Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics
5.1 PK/ADME
Absorption
In Vitro Permeability and Gastrointestinal Fluid Stability of Tradipitant (VLY-686)
According to the Biopharmaceutics Classification System (BCS) Guidelines Issued by
the United States FDA, the EMA, and the ICH
20VNDAP1GLPS607
In this two-part study, the in vitro permeability of tradipitant (named VLY-686 in this
report) was evaluated in a validated Caco-2 cell monolayer model, and the stability of
tradipitant in simulated gastric fluid (SGF) and simulated intestinal fluid (SIF) was
determined according to the Biopharmaceutics Classification System (BCS). Samples
were assayed using LC-MS/MS.
The permeability of tradipitant was higher than that of the positive control, minoxidil. As
minoxidil is greater than 85% absorbed in humans, it was inferred that tradipitant should
have greater than 85% absorption in humans. Therefore, tradipitant was classified as
highly permeable according to the BCS criteria.
Table 9. Unidirectional (A-to-B) Permeability and Recovery of Tradipitant and
Reference Compounds

VLY-686: tradipitant.
Table copied directly from study report (Study 20VNDAP1GLPS607, Table 1).

Based on the efflux ratio of 0.586, tradipitant was considered to permeate Caco-2 cell
monolayers primarily by passive diffusion and was not considered a substrate of efflux
transporters.

Table copied directly from study report (Study 20VNDAP1GLPS607, Table 2).

More than 90% of the nominal concentration of tradipitant remained after incubation for
60 minutes in SGF and 180 minutes in SIF. Based on these results, tradipitant was
considered stable in SGF and SIF.

Distribution
VLY-686 and Major Metabolites M2, M3 and M4: Investigation of Plasma Protein
Binding In Vitro in Rat, Mouse and Human by Rapid Equilibrium Dialysis
WW85WJ
The plasma protein binding of tradipitant (named VLY-686 in this report) and its
metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were investigated using plasma samples from rat, mouse,
and human. Tested concentrations were 300, 3000, and 30,000 ng/mL.
Tradipitant was highly bound in plasma in all species tested (≥ 99% bound in rat, ≥
98.5% in mouse, and ≥ 99.9% in human). Tradipitant concentration had no effect on
plasma protein binding in any species.
Metabolites M2, M3, and M4 were highly bound in plasma in all species tested (≥ 92.8%
bound in rat, ≥ 97.4% in mouse, and ≥ 95.4% in human). Metabolite concentration had
no effect on plasma protein binding in any species.

Metabolism
UGT Reaction Phenotyping of Tradipitant
XT164094
The UDP-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT) enzymes involved in the metabolism of
tradipitant to metabolite M8 (N-glucuronide) were investigated using human liver
microsomes and recombinant human UGT enzymes. In one experiment, tradipitant (0.5,
5, or 50 μM) was incubated with pooled human liver microsomes and with or without the
cofactor, uridine diphosphate glucuronic acid (UDPGA), for up to 120 minutes. In a
second experiment, tradipitant (5 or 50 µM) was incubated with recombinant human
enzymes UGT1A1, 1A3, 1A4, 1A6, 1A9, 2B7, 2B10, or 2B15, each for 60 minutes. In
both experiments, the amount of M8 was determined by LC-MS/MS.
69
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

In a preliminary experiment in which tradipitant was incubated with human liver
microsomes to assess the general metabolic potential of tradipitant into M8, loss of
tradipitant ranged from 0-17% in the presence of protein (liver microsomes) and
cofactor (UDPGA). Overall, metabolite M8 was inconsistently formed, but M8 formation
generally increased with incubation time, protein and substrate concentration, and
required the presence of both protein and UDPGA cofactor.

of Tradipitant to M8 by Human Liver Microsomes

any, UGT enzymes are responsible for the metabolism of tradipitant to M8, conversion
of tradipitant to M8 was observed with UGT1A4 at 5 and 50 μM tradipitant and with

Drug Interactions (Nonclinical)
In Vitro Interaction of LY686017 with Human Cytochromes P450 CYP3A, CYP2D6,
CYP2C19, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2
21-250-TC, 22-250-TC
The ability of tradipitant (named LY686017 in this report) to inhibit the activity of select
cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes was evaluated using human liver microsomes.
Metabolism of the appropriate substrate was evaluated by LC-MS/MS.
Tradipitant competitively inhibited CYP3A activity with a Ki of 0.92 μM and noncompetitively inhibited CYP3A activity with a Ki of 11 μM. Tradipitant competitively
71
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

inhibited both CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 activity with Ki values of 9.0 μM and 4.7 μM,
respectively. Additionally, tradipitant non-competitively inhibited CYP2C8 activity with a
Ki value of 17 μM. No significant effects were observed on CYP1A2, CYP2D6, CYP2E1,
or CYP2B6 activity over the tested concentration range.

Effects of LY686017 on CYP1A2 and CYP3A in Primary Cultures of Human
Hepatocytes
2003IV-HI02
The ability of tradipitant (named LY686017 in this report) to induce the activity of
CYP1A2 and CYP3A4 was evaluated using primary human hepatocytes. Hepatocytes
(n = 5 donors) were treated for 72 hours with tradipitant over the concentration range of
0.084-8.4 µM. Metabolism of the appropriate substrate was determined by UV detection
or LC-MS/MS.
Although the effects of tradipitant on CYP1A2 activity were variable, no biologically
significant induction was observed in any donor tested.
Changes in CYP3A activity were also variable. Tradipitant significantly increased
CYP3A4 activity in one donor each at 2.5 and 8.4 µM. Thus, tradipitant was considered
a potential inducer of CYP3A4 activity.

In Vitro Interaction of LY686017 with CYP2E1, CYP2C8, and CYP2B6 in Human Liver
Microsomes
CD-0822
The ability of tradipitant (named LY686017 in this report) to inhibit the activity of
CYP2E1, CYP2C8, and CYP2B6 was evaluated using human liver microsomes. Liver
microsomes were incubated for 10-20 minutes with tradipitant at concentrations up to
20 µM. Metabolism of the appropriate substrate was determined by LC-MS/MS.

Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Tradipitant non-competitively inhibited CYP2C8 activity with an apparent Ki value of 17
μM. Similar to the findings in Study 21-250-TC/22-250-TC, no significant inhibition (<
50%) of CYP2E1 and CYP2B6 activities was observed with tradipitant up to the highest
tested concentration of 20 µM.

Tradipitant: UDP-glucuronosyltransferase Inhibition in Human Liver Microsomes
XT205023
The ability of tradipitant to inhibit UGT1A4 was evaluated using human liver
microsomes. Liver microsomes were incubated with tradipitant over the concentration
range of 0.001-1 μM for 5 minutes, with reactions initiated every 10 seconds by the
addition of the cofactor UDPGA. Reactions were analyzed by LC-MS/MS.
No significant inhibition of UGT1A4 was observed with tradipitant.

Results
Metabolite M2
Cytotoxicity, demonstrated by a reduction in revertant colony numbers, was observed in
strain WP2 uvrA following exposure to M2 at 1500 μg/plate in the presence of S9. No
signs of cytotoxicity were observed with M2 under other tested conditions. Precipitate
was observed with M2 at ≥ 500 μg/plate in the absence of S9 mix and at ≥ 1500
μg/plate in the presence of S9 mix. No biologically significant increases in the number of
revertant colonies were observed with M2 up to 5000 µg/plate in the presence or
absence of S9 mix. Thus, M2 was considered not genotoxic under the conditions of the
assay.

Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Metabolite M3
Cytotoxicity, demonstrated by a thinning of the background lawn of non-revertant
colonies, was observed in strains TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537 following
exposure M3 at ≥ 500 μg/plate and in strain WP2 uvrA at ≥ 1500 μg/plate in the
presence of S9 mix. No signs of cytotoxicity were observed with M3 under other tested
conditions. Precipitate was observed with M3 at ≥ 500 μg/plate in the absence of S9 mix
and at ≥ 1500 μg/plate in the presence of S9 mix. No biologically significant increases in
the number of revertant colonies were observed with M3 up to 5000 µg/plate in the
presence or absence of S9 mix. Thus, M3 was considered not genotoxic under the
conditions of the assay.
Metabolite M4
Cytotoxicity, demonstrated by a reduction in revertant colony numbers, was observed in
strain TA98 following exposure to M4 at 500 and 1500 μg/plate in the absence of S9
mix. No signs of cytotoxicity were observed with M4 under other tested conditions.
Precipitate was observed on all plates containing M4 at ≥ 500 μg/plate in the absence
and presence of S9 mix. No biologically significant increases in the number of revertant
colonies were observed with M4 up to 5000 µg/plate in the presence or absence of S9
mix. Thus, M4 was considered not genotoxic under the conditions of the assay.

10 Special Toxicology Studies
10.1 HSTOX 7619: Evaluating the safety and toxicology of tradipitant in
multi-organ, microphysiological system

(b) (4)

HSTOX 7619
Methods
Limited methods were provided in this study report. An information request was sent to
the Applicant on March 27, 2024, requesting further detail on various aspects of the
study design and conduct. Information provided by the Applicant in their response
received March 26, 2024, is included in the description of the methods below.19
(b) (4) multiThe potential toxic effects of tradipitant were evaluated using
organ MPS. This custom, human-based multi-organ platform was composed of liver
(primary biopsy), cardiac (induced pluripotent stem cell [iPSC]-derived), kidney (primary
biopsy), and neuronal (iPSC-derived) “organ” modules and was referred to as the “fourorgan system.”

Initial experiments evaluated the stability of tradipitant in the four-organ system over a
7-day period. Additionally, the pharmacokinetic profiles of tradipitant and metabolites
M2, M3, and M4 were determined. Samples were collected at 1, 4, 8, 24, and 48 hours
and analyzed via HPLC-MS (n = 3 for each time point and concentration). Data were fit
to a PK model incorporating mixing and loss of compound to adsorption; no further
information was provided on this PK modeling.
To evaluate the toxicity of tradipitant, the system was treated once daily for 7 days with
vehicle or tradipitant (Lot # RS-IN-VDDE17022) at 3 or 100 µM. According to the
Applicant, these concentrations were selected to achieve “plasma” exposures of
approximately 500 and 3500 ng/mL, respectively. The vehicle (not defined) was used as
the negative control, and no positive controls were used for any assay.
Reviewer note: The low and high concentrations (plasma exposures) used in this study
represent tradipitant exposure at approximately 1.5- and 11-fold the clinical Cmax of 327
ng/mL, respectively, as determined in healthy volunteers following 12 days of dosing at
85 mg BID. The Applicant did not conduct IVIVE analyses to determine how the in vitro
concentration relates to that observed in humans. Thus, the stated effect margins
should be interpreted with caution, and all observed effects are discussed in terms of
absolute concentration.
Unless otherwise noted, endpoints evaluating cellular function were measured pretreatment (with tradipitant being added to the culture immediately after baseline
recordings) and following 1, 4, and 7 days of treatment. Each condition was tested in
duplicate per experiment, and four biological replicates were used for each assay.
Statistical analysis methods were not provided.
(b) (4)

77
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

Hepatocytes
Primary human hepatocytes were obtained from two donors. Both Donors 1 and 2 were
used to evaluate the metabolism of tradipitant in the four-organ system, while only
Donor 1 was used for the main experiments evaluating hepatocyte function and toxicity.
Primary human hepatocytes from Donor 1 were purchased from the

(b) (4)

This donor
was a 44-year-old Caucasian female with a body mass index (BMI) of 26.4. No other
information on the health status of the donor was provided.
(b) (4)
Primary human hepatocytes from Donor 1 were purchased from
. This donor was a 42-year-old African American
male, with a BMI of 31, who used alcohol approximately twice per month. The donor
was on multiple medications including aspirin 81 mg, metopolol 25 mg, Toviaz 4 mg,
and botox injections in legs and bladder. The cause of death of this donor was stroke.

Hepatocytes were cultured on coverslips derivatized with an amine-containing silane.
Additionally, Kasumi-1 and MEG-01 cells were suspended in alginate-based hydrogels
in separate chambers. The purpose of the Kasumi-1 and MEG-01 cells was not
provided by the Applicant or in their referenced publication.20 Thus, the contribution of
these other cell types to the function of this assay is unclear.
In this study, hepatocyte function was evaluated based on CYP3A4 activity, viability,
and albumin production. CYP3A4 enzymatic activity was assessed in the liver fraction
using a chemiluminescent assay (Promega p450-610TM assay), according to the
manufacturer’s instructions.21 Cell viability was assessed after disassembly via a
standard 3-(4,5-dimethylthiazol-z-yl)-2,5- diphenyltetrazolium bromide (MTT) assay.20
Human serum albumin production from the liver fraction was measured in the
supernatant of the culture. Samples were collected from the exchanged medium and
stored at −20°C until analysis. One freeze-thaw cycle occurred before samples were
analyzed following manufacturers’ instructions provided in the ELISA Starter Accessory
Kit. Samples were diluted as necessary in sample conjugate/diluent buffer to ensure
readings measured within the range of the standard curve.21
Cardiomyocytes
iPSC-derived cardiomyocytes used in this study were purchased from
(b) (4) The source of human
fibroblasts used to derive these cells was a healthy female Caucasian donor less than
18 years of age.
Donor-derived human fibroblasts were reprogrammed to iPSCs through ectopic
expression of factors (i.e., Oct4, Sox2, Nanog, Lin28) by retroviral transduction. The
retroviral particles used in this process were obtained from the cell culture supernatant
of HEK 293T cells transfected with plasmids containing the reprogramming factor genes
as well as the coding regions of the gag, pol, and env retroviral genes. No retroviral

78
Reference ID: 5719891

NDA # 220152

Sarah J. Morgan, PhD

gene expression was detected by PCR in the starting fibroblast material or the resulting
cell line, confirming the cell line cannot spontaneously produce infectious virus.
The iPSC-derived cell clones (cardiomyocytes) were engineered through homologous
recombination to exhibit blasticidin resistance under the control of a cardiomyocytespecific promotor. Neomycin resistance was also included in the targeting vector to
allow selection of clones. None of the engineering vectors used contained oncogenes.
The final cell population contained ≥ 95% cardiomyocytes. Whole-genome transcript
profiling and immunocytochemical characterization demonstrated that cardiomyocytes,
at the time of purchase, exhibited a stable human cardiac gene expression profile with
proper protein expression and localization necessary for cardiac function.
iPSC-derived cardiomyocytes obtained from the manufacturer were thawed and

Cardiac electrical function was measured by incorporating and chemically patterning

deflection of the cantilever due to contractions resulted in changes in position of the
reflected beam on the lateral effect sensor.21,24,25 The voltage output from the position
sensor was converted directly to force using a modified form of Stoney’s equation, as
